*The Prophet Jonah cried aloud, prefiguring the three-day burial, praying in the belly of the whale: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!*[^4790]

*Troparion 1:* Shine forth upon me the dawn of repentance, O Lady, and dispel the clouds of my wicked thoughts, O cloud...[^4791]

...of the Sun of righteousness, O Ever-Virgin.[^4792]
*Troparion 2:* Still the fierce wave of my passions, and calm the tempest of wicked thoughts, O great haven of the tempest-tossed, Ever-Virgin.[^4793]
*Troparion 3:* Give me to drink of the drink of compunction, O Lady, granting me now rivers of tears, wherewith I may quench the eternal flame, O only all-hymned one.[^4794]
*Troparion 4:* Drying up the fierce deep of my sins, O thou who truly didst bear the deep of goodness, guide me unto the haven of the divine will.[^4795]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The godly-minded worshiped not the creature rather than the Creator, but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!*[^4796]

*Troparion 1:* The adversary was conquered and fell with a marvelous fall, when Christ was lifted up upon the Tree; and he who was formerly condemned was saved, crying unto Him: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers![^4797]
*Troparion 2:* Having died upon the Tree, O Christ, Thou didst make alive me who was dead through the tree, and by Thy divine wounds didst heal the sores of my heart: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers![^4798]
*Martyricon 1:* Having received the gift of healings, to heal infirmities and drive away demons from men by spiritual power, heal the passions of my heart by your prayers, O unconquerable Martyrs.[^4799]
*Martyricon 2:* Enduring drowning, the tormentor perished with his hosts by your blood; while ye, O all-praised Martyrs, rejoicing cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers![^4800]
*Stavrotheotokion:* O spotless Bride, palace of the Creator, untilled earth, fiery-visaged throne, Thou wast revealed, O All-Pure One! Therefore we cry unto thee: Rejoice, all-pure Lady, who hast deified men by thy divine childbirth.[^4801]

*Another [Canon], Same Heirmos:*
*Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice justly ensnared: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers!*[^4802]

*Troparion 1:* With much love I flee beneath thy holy protection: turn me not away into darkness, but grant...[^4803]

...unto me, O All-Pure One, the remission of transgressions, and save me, that mine enemies, seeing it, may be put to shame.[^4804]
*Troparion 2:* I will fear no evil, for thou art with me, O Virgin! Drive away the enemies who mercilessly persecute me, and being strengthened by thy power I will conquer them, O Mary, Birthgiver of God.[^4805]
*Troparion 3:* Thou hast power always, as having given birth unto the Master of all; free me from the dominion of pleasure and passions, that rejoicing I may sing: Rejoice, throne of the Most High, O blessed one![^4806]
*Troparion 4:* Thee alone do I, thy servant, have as protectress on earth, and as a help truly saving and firm, O All-Pure One, glorified of God! And unto thee do I flee: save me from the snares of hunters, O Birthgiver of God.[^4807]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Offspring of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace; then prefigured, but now in act, He gathers the whole universe to sing to Thee: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.*[^4808]

*Troparion 1:* Thou releasest me from the bonds of time, having come into time though timeless; and being bound willingly, O Master, Thou didst deliver the proud one unto indissoluble bonds, and savest me by Thy Cross and Passion. Therefore do I bless Thee, O Christ, unto the ages.[^4809]
*Troparion 2:* Lifted up upon the Tree of Thine own will, Thou didst raise all creation with Thyself, O all-hymned, unoriginate, and invisible Word; and by Thy Passion, O Christ, Thou didst expose the principalities and powers of darkness; therefore do we hymn Thee unto all ages.[^4810]
*Martyricon 1:* Ye mounted upon your blood as upon a chariot, O all-comely Martyrs, and were taken up unto the supermundane dwellings, receiving worthy honors from Christ, and crying aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages![^4811]
*Martyricon 2:* Lifted up upon trees, cast into the pit, delivered unto beasts, divided into fire and water, the passion-bearing Martyrs rejoicing sang: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages![^4812]
*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Christ fallen asleep upon the Tree, Who grants unto all divine and saving wakefulness, the all-immaculate Mother with weeping cried aloud and exclaimed: What is this newest marvel? Doth He Who gives life unto all willingly die?[^4813]

*Another [Canon], Same Heirmos:*
*Troparion 1:* Having lived my life prodigally, and having committed every uncleanness, I tremble before the judgment-seat, I tremble at the interrogation, I tremble at the answer of my condemnation, O Pure One! O thou who didst bear the Judge, stand before me then, and deliver me from distress.[^4814]
*Troparion 2:* Upon thee have I placed all the hope of my salvation, O Mother of God who knewest not wedlock, and thee do I ever call upon for help: save me from the griefs and assaults of the enemy, and loose the bonds of my sins, and snatch me from eternal darkness.[^4815]
*Troparion 3:* In the hour of my end, O Virgin, snatch me from the hands of demons, and from judgment, and accusation, and the fearful examination, and the bitter toll-houses, and the cruel prince, O Mother of God, and from eternal condemnation.[^4816]
*Troparion 4:* Preserve thy servant, O Virgin, from every assault of the stranger; for thee do I have, O Lady, as protection and intercession, refuge and confirmation; and for thy sake I hope to be delivered from the snares of the enemy, O only Protectress of the human race.[^4817]

*My soul doth magnify the Lord: with prostrations.*[^4818]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit, and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry aloud: Rejoice, all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!*[^4819]

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst stand to be judged, O Lover of mankind, Who art to judge all; and with a crown of thorns Thou wast voluntarily bound, O Savior, of Thine own will, tearing up by the root, O Christ, the thorns of disobedience, and planting in all the knowledge of Thy tender mercy.[^4820]
*Troparion 2:* O how the lawless people, being darkened by envy, deliver Thee, the righteous and blameless Giver of light, unto the Cross! Beholding Whose Passion, the sun was darkened, and the brightness of the temple was rent, and the foundations of the earth quaked.[^4821]
*Martyricon 1:* Ye were made conformed unto the Passions of Christ, O holy Martyrs, and heirs of the kingdom and of brightness; therefore enlighten your singers, O most wise ones, delivering them from sinful gloom and diverse tribulations.[^4822]
*Martyricon 2:* Having already settled into the very heavenly realms, O most wise ones,...[^4823]

...and having received ever-existing glory, and being deified by sacred communion, remember all of us who by faith honor your all-sacred and honorable memory, O ever-glorious ones.[^4824]
*Theotokion:* Enlighten, O Pure One, those who with love praise and magnify thee; dispel the darkness of our passions, O Maiden; calm the storm, and drive away from our midst the temptations of the wicked one; and subdue the children of Hagar, O Maiden, unto our pious Emperor (*Name*)[^4825], by thy prayers.[^4826]

*Another [Canon], Same Heirmos:*
*Troparion 1:* Rejoice, O All-Pure One, who truly didst bear joy unto those upon earth! Rejoice, saving haven and protection of those fleeing unto thee! Rejoice, pure ladder who didst raise the fallen! Rejoice, all-blessed Mother of God, the hope of our souls![^4827]
*Troparion 2:* Destroy the pasture of sin of my defiled soul and body, O Lady graced of God, all-pure one, by thy mighty prayer, O All-Immaculate One, granting saving healing, the divine fear of the Master.[^4828]
*Troparion 3:* Thou art my illumination, thou art my deliverance and rejoicing, thou art my helper, thou art my glory and boast, and the expectation of my salvation, O All-Immaculate One! And unto thee do I faithfully bow down, and cry unto thee: Save me, thy wretched servant, and snatch me from the gates of hades.[^4829]
*Troparion 4:* Save me, O Pure One, who didst bear the all-compassionate Savior; show mercy unto me, thy servant, and guide me unto the way of repentance; drive away from our midst the temptations of the wicked one, and deliver me from his snaring, and snatch me from eternal fire, O All-Undefiled One.[^4830]

*It is truly meet: Litany, Exapostilarion, and the usual Psalms.*[^4831]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 4:*
*Sticheron 1:* Be a wall unto us, Thy Cross, O Jesus our Savior! For no other hope do we the faithful possess save Thee, Who wast nailed thereon in the flesh, and grantest us great mercy.[^4832]
*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad... And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their sons [LXX Ps. 89:14, 16].*[^4833]
*Sticheron 2:* Thou hast given a sign unto them that fear Thee, O Lord: Thy precious Cross,...[^4834]

...wherewith Thou didst put to shame the principalities and powers of darkness, and didst bring us back unto the first blessedness; therefore we glorify Thy dispensation, Lover of mankind, O almighty Jesus, Savior of our souls.[^4835]
*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and direct the works of our hands upon us; yea, direct Thou the work of our hands [LXX Ps. 89:17].*[^4836]
*Martyricon:* Who doth not marvel, beholding, O holy Martyrs, the good fight which ye fought? How, being in the flesh, ye overcame the incorporeal enemy, confessing Christ, and being armed with the Cross? Therefore were ye worthily shown forth as expellers of demons and conquerors of barbarians, unceasingly praying that our souls be saved.[^4837]
*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^4838]
When the Ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, She marveled, and cried aloud: What is this vision, O beloved Son? Hath the unfaithful and lawless synagogue rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master![^4839]

*It is good to give praise: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparion, litany, and the First Hour, and the rest as usual, and the dismissal.*[^4840]

*On Friday at the Liturgy, Tone 4.*
*The Beatitudes:*
*Troparion 1:* Through a tree Adam became an exile from Paradise, but through the tree of the Cross the thief entered to dwell in Paradise: for the one, having tasted, cast away the commandment of Him that made him, while the other, being crucified with Thee, confessed the hidden God, crying aloud: Remember me in Thy kingdom![^4841]
*Troparion 2:* Beholding Thee stretched out upon the Cross, O Thou Who alone art longsuffering, the heavenly powers, being at a loss, marveled with trembling; and the earth quaked, and the beauty of the luminaries was quenched; when Thou, O Lover of mankind, wast unrighteously condemned, Adam who was condemned was justified: I glorify Thy tender mercy.[^4842]
*Troparion 3:* Lifted up upon Golgotha, Thou didst crush the head of the enemy; and having died upon the Tree, O Master, Thou didst make alive those who died through the ancient fruit, and didst reveal them as dwellers of Paradise, unceasingly glorifying Thy goodness and crying aloud: Remember us in Thy kingdom![^4843]
*Martyricon:* Having taken up the Cross as a weapon, O holy Martyrs, with valiant purpose ye went forth unto the battle array of the enemies; and having destroyed them, with an incorruptible crown...[^4844]

...ye were crowned, and obtained highest glory, rejoicing, O richly-blessed ones; therefore with faith we call you blessed.[^4845]
*Glory...:* That Thou mightest manifestly show forth, O Savior, Thy tender mercy toward us, Thou wast nailed upon the Cross, Who art united unto the Father and the Spirit; Thou didst endure the sponge and reed, mockings and wounds, desiring to deliver from eternal fire those who cry: Remember us, O Savior, in Thy kingdom![^4846]
*Both now...:* He Who cannot be contained anywhere, unconfined by space, took up His abode within thy holy womb, O Birthgiver of God, all-pure Lady! And suspended upon the Tree, He manifestly poured forth life unto the world. Entreat Him, as Lover of mankind, to mortify the carnal mind of our flesh, and to save us all.[^4847]

## Saturday: All Saints and the Departed[^4848]

*On Friday Evening.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerables, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^4849]

*Sticheron 1:* Zealous for the Passions of Christ, the Lover of mankind, O passion-bearers, ye surrendered your bodies unto wounds, and bitter torments, and myriads of pains, as ever foreseeing the divine delight of Paradise, the inexhaustible food, and the eternal good glory; which having obtained, pray ye for those who praise you.[^4850]
*At the second verse, say the same sticheron again.*[^4851]
*Sticheron 2:* All-sacred shepherds, glorious imitators of Christ the Chief Shepherd, King of all, ye zealously laid down your lives for the sheep, and endured cruel tribulations, as assaulted by grievous wolves, O richly-blessed ones; and ye preserve unharmed the God-chosen flock.[^4852]

*Other Stichera to the Martyrs, same Tone and Podoben (то́йже подо́бенъ):*
*Sticheron 3:* Living sacrifices, rational whole-burnt offerings, Martyrs of the Lord, perfect slaughters unto God, sheep knowing God and known of God, whose fold is inaccessible to wolves: pray for us, that we also may be pastured with you by the water of rest.[^4853]
*Sticheron 4:* Precious is the death of Thy saints, O Lord; for by the sword and fire, and with a contrite soul,...[^4854]

...they shed their blood, having hope in Thee, to receive the reward of their labors; and having endured, they received from Thee, O Savior, great mercy.[^4855]
*Sticheron 5:* Having boldness toward the Savior, O holy ones, pray unceasingly for us sinners, asking remission of transgressions and great mercy for our souls.[^4856]
*Glory... Both now... Dogmatic Theotokion:*
David, the forefather of God, through thee, O Prophet, musically proclaimed concerning thee unto Him Who hath done great things for thee: The queen stood at Thy right hand. For God, Who was well-pleased without a father to become man of thee, revealed thee as Mother and mediatress of life, that He might renew His own image, corrupted through passions; and having found the sheep that went astray upon the mountains, taking it upon His shoulder, He will bring it unto the Father, and according to His own will will unite it unto the heavenly Powers, and Christ, Who hath great and rich mercy, will save the world, O Mother of God.[^4857]

*O Gladsome Light: Prokeimenon of the day, Vouchsafe, O Lord: And the litany.*[^4858]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera to the Martyrs, Tone 4:*
*Sticheron 1:* O Christ God, Who art glorified in the memorials of Thy saints, and being entreated by them, send down upon us great mercy.[^4859]
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven: - - - Even so our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have mercy upon us [LXX Ps. 122:1-2].*[^4860]
*Sticheron 2:* O Lover of mankind, Who didst accept the endurance of the holy martyrs, accept also from us our singing, and grant us through their prayers great mercy.[^4861]
*Verse: Have mercy upon us, O Lord, have mercy upon us, for we are utterly filled with contempt. Our soul is exceedingly filled with the reproach of the prosperous, and with the contempt of the proud [LXX Ps. 122:3-4].*[^4862]
*For the Departed:*
*Sticheron 3:* With the spirits of the righteous who have been perfected, give rest unto the souls of Thy servants, O Savior, preserving them in the blessed life which is with Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^4863]
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Sticheron 4:* O unquenchable lamp, righteous throne, all-pure Lady, pray that our souls be saved.[^4864]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparion, and dismissal.*[^4865]

*On Friday at Compline, Tone 4.*
*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*O Thou Who wast born of a Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the tripartite power of my soul in the depth of dispassion, as of old Thou didst drown the mighty captains, that in the mortification of the body, as upon a timbrel, I may sing unto Thee a song of victory.*[^4866]

*Refrain: Most holy Lady Mother of God, save us.*[^4867]
*Troparion 1:* From the mire of passions, from the tempest of thoughts, from the darts of the wicked one, and from every assault of the adversary, save my wretched soul, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, and snatch me from eternal fire.[^4868]
*Troparion 2:* Lead me up, O Pure One, from the depth of transgressions, and guide me unto the light of the divine commandments of the Savior Christ and God; shine forth upon me the saving dawn of repentance, and grant me life eternal.[^4869]
*Glory...:* Having fallen into the evil mire of my sins, I am sorely choked by evil deeds, and have no stability: O Lady Virgin Mother, by thy power lead me up, and deliver me from fire and torment.[^4870]
*Both now...:* Death and corruption hast thou abolished, O all-hymned Mother of God, having given birth unto Christ the fountain of incorruption, Who adorned human nature with immortality and grace, O All-Undefiled One.[^4871]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.*[^4872]

*Troparion 1:* Having appeared, O pure Virgin, take away the darkness of my soul, and rend asunder the bonds of sin, and save me, I pray thee, by thine intercession.[^4873]
*Troparion 2:* My heart, shaken by passionate assaults, establish in the most pure fear of God, O all-undefiled Virgin, by thy tender mercy.[^4874]
*Glory...:* Despairing of all help, unto thee, O All-Undefiled One, as mighty guardian and protection, have I run: cast me not away, therefore, from thy divine protection.[^4875]
*Both now...:* Arrayed in fringes of gold as a queen, at the right hand of the King and God thou standest now, O Lady, praying for thy servants.[^4876]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine came upon a light cloud, and with His incorrupt hand He saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!*[^4877]

*Troparion 1:* Deliver me who am held fast by despondency, completely darkened by the gloom of passions, and enslaved to sin, O divinely-blessed Maiden, and unite me unto Thy Son and our God.[^4878]

*Troparion 2:* Mortified by the malice of the deceiver, O All-Undefiled One, as having given birth unto Life, make me alive, for unto thee have I fled; and lead me up who am plunged into the depth of downfalls, as thou art merciful, O All-Blameless One.[^4879]
*Glory...:* In the grave of mine immeasurable pleasures I lie low, and am held fast by despondency and sloth; but O thou who gavest birth unto the Resurrection of all, make me alive, O Good One, and save me.[^4880]
*Both now...:* The prophet of old called thee a sacred and divine mountain, O pure Virgin, overshadowed all round by virtues, from which the saving Word appeared, for the renewal and illumination of our souls.[^4881]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ, but we, rising early from the night, hymn Thee, the Only-begotten, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind.*[^4882]

*Troparion 1:* That my humble soul may rejoice, O Lady, to fear Thy Son, and to do His commandments from the heart, guide me, O all-undefiled and Most Pure One, by thy prayers.[^4883]
*Troparion 2:* With all my heart, O good Lady, vouchsafe my passionate soul to confess unto the Redeemer, driving away the ignorance of my heart, O All-Undefiled One.[^4884]
*Glory...:* Preserve my soul as the apple of the eye under the shelter of thy wings, O good and Most Pure One, and deliver me from the torment and torture of evil spirits.[^4885]
*Both now...:* Reveal thyself delivering thy servant from the assault of passions and from evil demons, O Most Pure One, who hast gained thee as a mighty protection and an unashamed advocate.[^4886]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The prophet Jonah, praying in the whale, cried aloud, prefiguring the three-day burial: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!*[^4887]

*Troparion 1:* Cast down those who ever wage war against me, O Lady, and disperse the cloud of evil thoughts, for thou art the gate of the Sun of righteousness, O Mother of God.[^4888]
*Troparion 2:* Cause the dawn of repentance to shine upon me, O Lady, and dispel the clouds of my wicked thoughts, drawn away and corrupted as I am by cruel enticements.[^4889]
*Glory...:* Still the raging wave of my passions, and destroy the tempest of wicked thoughts, O steadfast protectress and shelter of all, O All-Undefiled One.[^4890]

*Both now...:* The transgressions of my soul are multiplied above the sand of the sea, and as a heavy burden they weigh me down; but do thou, O Virgin, having compassion on me before the end, save me.[^4891]
*Lord, have mercy (3). Glory... Both now...*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
As a fervent protection in tribulations, and our helper, and reconciliation unto God, through whom we were delivered from corruption, we, the faithful, call the Mother of God blessed.[^4892]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Abrahamic youths of old in Babylon trampled down the flame of the furnace, crying aloud in hymns: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*[^4893]

*Troparion 1:* Thee, O pure Mother of God, do I faithfully invoke: preserve my humble heart, and deliver me from the flame of torment and eternal darkness.[^4894]
*Troparion 2:* Rend asunder, O pure Virgin, with the spear of Thy Son the handwriting of my transgressions, and deliver me, I pray thee, from all possession at the hour of my departure.[^4895]
*Glory...:* Quench, O pure Virgin, with the streams of my tears and the rain of thy compassions, the furnace which the multitude of my wicked deeds hath prepared for me by demonic working.[^4896]
*Both now...:* Deliver us from the assaults of temptations, O only Mother of the Word and Ever-Virgin, and from every torment, who truly and understandingly hymn thee unto all ages.[^4897]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*O all-powerful Redeemer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew the godly youths and teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and praise the Lord!*[^4898]

*Troparion 1:* Heal my ailing soul, and enlighten my darkened mind, and snatch me from fire and eternal, ineffable torments, O Mother of God, Virgin Bride of God.[^4899]
*Troparion 2:* Bowed down by irrational passions, I have descended into the depth of perdition, and have made myself burning fuel for the flame; from which deliver me, O Virgin Mother of God, thy servant.[^4900]
*Glory...:* Dry up the deep of my wicked deeds, O thou who gavest birth unto the Abyss of compassion, the Redeemer and Lord; and loose the chains of my wicked deeds before the end, O thou who gavest birth unto the Savior of all.[^4901]
*Both now...:* The Lord Who rules over creation, having become incarnate from thy womb, revealed thee, O Virgin, as Sovereign Lady over all things both visible and invisible,...[^4902]

...glorifying thee as His undefiled Mother.[^4903]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse, but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the bud of thy childbearing didst blossom forth blessing unto the world: therefore do we all magnify thee.*[^4904]

*Troparion 1:* My heart, which hath been barren of divine virtues, do thou, O most pure Virgin Mother of God, show forth to be fruitful, O thou who wast born of a barren woman by the will and beck of Him Who transforms all things, that I may hymn thee, the All-Praised.[^4905]
*Troparion 2:* With thy light enlighten me, O All-Undefiled One, who gavest birth unto the unapproachable Light; drive away the clouds of my passions, and snatch me from murky darkness, and vouchsafe me divine light, that I may hymn thee, O Virgin Mother.[^4906]
*Glory...:* By the sprinkling of thy compassions, O Pure One, wash away the ulcers of my heart, and grant me ever to pour forth streams of tears, subduing the torrents of passions, and delivering me from torment.[^4907]
*Both now...:* O gracious Mother of God, who gavest birth unto God Who loves what is good, quickly deliver me now from evil carnal love, O Lady, and enslave me unto the divine will, who am perishing through sloth.[^4908]

*It is truly meet: and a prostration. Trisagion. After Our Father: troparia and the rest as usual.*[^4909]

*On Saturday at Matins,*[^4910]
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymns to the Martyrs, Tone 4:*
*Sessional Hymn 1:* Today the angelic hosts have come to the memorial of the passion-bearers, to enlighten the minds of the faithful and brighten the universe with grace; being entreated by them, O God, grant us great mercy.[^4911]
*Verse: God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:36].*[^4912]
*Sessional Hymn 2:* Armed with the Cross, Thy passion-bearers, O Christ our God, conquered the wiles of the author of evil, the enemy; they shone forth as luminaries instructing men, and grant healings unto those who ask in faith: by their supplications save our souls.[^4913]
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown unto the angels, through thee, O Mother of God, was revealed unto those upon earth: God in unconfused union being in-...[^4914]

...carnate, and voluntarily accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby He raised the first-fashioned man, and saved our souls from death.[^4915]

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymns, Tone 4:*
*Sessional Hymn 1:* Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors and crushed the impotent insolence of demons: by their supplications save our souls.[^4916]
*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, but out of them all the Lord will deliver them [LXX Ps. 33:20].*[^4917]
*Sessional Hymn 2:* Adorned with the blood of Thy martyrs throughout the whole world as with purple and fine linen, Thy Church cries out through them unto Thee, O Christ God: Send down Thy compassions upon Thy people, grant peace unto Thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy.[^4918]
*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord, and their memorial is from generation to generation [LXX Ps. 64:5, 101:13].*[^4919]
*For the Departed:*
*Sessional Hymn 3:* The souls which Thou hast translated from temporal things, O Savior, give rest as Thou art tender-hearted and all-powerful, and the only Lover of mankind; forgive as Thou art compassionate the things committed by them, and have mercy, O Merciful One, upon the works of Thy hands, by the intercessions of the Mother of God, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind.[^4920]
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure, all-undefiled, and unwedded Virgin, who alone didst bear in time the timeless Son and Word of God: entreat Him together with the holy and honorable patriarchs, martyrs, prophets, and venereables, to grant us purification and great mercy.[^4921]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Prophets, and Venerables.*
*(Whose Acrostic is: Brightly do I praise the divine friends of Christ.)*
*A Composition of Joseph. Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*O Thou Who wast born of a Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the tripartite power of my soul in the depth of dispassion, as of old Thou didst drown the mighty captains, that in the mortification of the body, as upon a timbrel, I may sing unto Thee a song of victory.*[^4922]

*Troparion 1:* The honorable Church is ever made bright by the radiant struggles of the Lord's sufferers, and she glorifies Christ, the Sun Who shone forth from the Virgin and dispersed the darkness of deceit, honoring Him.[^4923]
*Troparion 2:* Let us faithfully praise Christ's excellent hierarchs, who well pastured the people; and brightly let us praise the entire assembly of those who...[^4924]

...lived monastically, and mortified bodily pleasures by the Spirit.[^4925]

*Troparion 3:* Strength from God was granted unto women, who in fasting and steadfast suffering trampled down the enemy by grace: by their prayers, O Lord, and of Thy holy prophets, send down Thy mercies upon all.[^4926]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
Justify, O Christ, Thy servants whom by righteous providence Thou hast translated from corruptible life unto incorruptible life; and vouchsafe them to join the chorus with all Thy righteous ones, overlooking their transgressions.[^4927]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Delivering us from the first condemnation, the Son of God was well pleased, O Pure One, to become also thy Son; therefore, having been adopted as sons for thy sake, we bless the heavenly Father, hymning thee.[^4928]

*Another Canon, for the Departed,*
*which we sing in order when there is no Menaion. (Whose Acrostic is: As a fourth rule, I commemorate the end).*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Israel of old, having traversed the Red Sea's deep with dryshod steps, by Moses' hands stretched out crosswise conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*[^4929]

*Refrain: God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:36].*[^4930]
*Troparion 1:* Holding Thy servants by the right hand, lead them unto the immortal pasture, O Savior, unto the contemplation of Thy beauty, being entreated by the passion-bearing martyrs.[^4931]
*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^4932]
*Troparion 2:* Vouchsafe those who have passed away in faith to inherit Thine ineffable and blessed glory, O Christ, overlooking their transgressions, and justifying them by grace through the shedding of Thy Blood.[^4933]
*Glory...:* By Thy lifegiving death, O Christ God, Thou didst mortify the slayer of those whom Thou hast received; granting them Thy life, do Thou Thyself now give rest unto Thy faithful servants.[^4934]
*Both now...:* Fair in transcendent beauties as the Son of God; yet as having become incarnate of thee, O Virgin, and suspended upon the Tree, He had no beauty, accepting death for all.[^4935]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with strength; therefore...*[^4936]

...my heart is established in the Lord.*[^4937]

*Troparion 1:* As lambs, the fair-victorious martyrs, having been slain, were offered unto Christ, the Lamb and Word of God Who was slain for the deliverance of all.[^4938]
*Troparion 2:* Having become the mouth of God, the initiates of sacred mysteries brought forth the worthy from the unworthy, enlightening them with sacred teachings.[^4939]
*Troparion 3:* The choir of the venereables found inexhaustible food through temperance, delighting in spiritual contemplations and divine ascents.[^4940]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
All those whom Thou hast translated in faith, O Christ, by the supplications of all Thy saints give rest, overlooking the transgressions committed by them in this life.[^4941]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Let us offer praise unto God, Who was born in the flesh of the Virgin, and Who joined the choirs of women unto the angelic choirs.[^4942]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.*[^4943]

*Troparion 1:* Having conquered the delusion of idols, the martyrs now entreat the Master and God to grant divine rest unto those who have departed before.[^4944]
*Troparion 2:* Be well-pleased, O Master, that Thy servants whom Thou hast translated may pass by the turning sword, and vouchsafe them to obtain excellent participation in the Tree of Life.[^4945]
*Glory...:* In Paradise, O Christ, vouchsafe Thy servants to dwell in delight, where the sound of those that keep festival is pure, granting them remission of sins.[^4946]
*Both now...:* Unto divine childbearing hast thou joined virginity, O Most Pure One; for ineffably didst thou bear the Creator of all things, unto Whose will all things submit.[^4947]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine came upon a light cloud, and with His incorrupt hand He saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!*[^4948]

*Troparion 1:* The most sacred assembly of martyrs hath been glorified, who sacredly glorified in their members the Lord Who is praised by all angels; and they pray that we may be delivered from every tribulation.[^4949]
*Troparion 2:* Having a mind radiant with immaterial light, O divine hierarchs, ye destroyed the night of all...[^4950]

...deceit, and by unerring guidance led the God-chosen flock of Christ unto understanding.[^4951]

*Troparion 3:* The multitude of venereables was adorned, and the unique triumph of holy women was magnified, and the holy company of prophets was honored, rejoicing as they joined with the angelic assemblies.[^4952]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
Having become fellow citizens with the angels, O holy martyrs, ask for all the departed relief, and dwelling in the divine Paradise, and final deliverance from transgressions.[^4953]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
He Who preserved thee an incorrupt Virgin after childbearing glorified all the virgins who surround thee. With them pray unceasingly, that our souls may be saved from every tribulation and distress.[^4954]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, fittingly crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*[^4955]

*Troparion 1:* The martyrs have appeared as luminaries, illuminating the firmament of the Church; and they entreat the Savior Christ to grant relief unto the departed.[^4956]
*Troparion 2:* Having Thy Cross as the rod of power, Thy servants passed through the worldly sea; settle them, O Lord, in Thy holy mountain, wherein Thou hast made a sanctuary.[^4957]
*Glory...:* In the truly beloved tabernacles, O Master, which Thou hast chosen, be well-pleased as Thou art merciful that Thy servants whom Thou hast received may dwell, where the spirits of the righteous abide.[^4958]
*Both now...:* Ruling over both the dead and the living, the Immortal One, having become incarnate as man from thee, O Mother of God, endured death in the flesh, destroying the power of death.[^4959]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as a light, a holy light turning those who hymn Thee in faith from dark ignorance.*[^4960]

*Troparion 1:* Lifted up upon crosses, crushed upon wheels, and cut into pieces in their limbs, the valiant martyrs were shown to be undivided from God.[^4961]
*Troparion 2:* Having become saviors unto the faithful, O hierarchs, ye brought unto the Lamb and Master those whom ye were piously entrusted to pasture.[^4962]
*Troparion 3:* Withdrawing from worldly tumults, O venereables, ye calmed your hearts...[^4963]

...from passions, and became sons unto the God of all.[^4964]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
O divine prophets and company of women, who of old were well-pleasing unto God, ask rest for the departed.[^4965]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
God, having dwelt within thee, O all-pure Virgin, made men to be the divine dwellings of His glory.[^4966]

*Another Heirmos, the same:*
*Thou, O Lord, by the might of Thy dominion hast glorified the martyrs: for their sake give rest unto those who have departed before, as Thou art tender-hearted.*[^4967]

*Troparion 1:* Grant indissoluble life, fair rejoicing, and unceasing gladness unto those who have died before, O Plenteous in mercy.[^4968]
*Glory...:* O Thou Who alone art good and the fountain of goodness, give rest unto those who have finished their lives in faith and in the knowledge of Thee.[^4969]
*Both now...:* We hymn thee, O Mother of God, through whom ineffable and unapproachable Light shone forth upon those in darkness, and in love we call thee blessed.[^4970]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I am come into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of many sins hath overwhelmed me; but as God, lead up my life from the depth, O Plenteous in mercy.*[^4971]

*Troparion 1:* As most precious stones wisely rolling upon the earth, O martyrs, ye threw down the entire edifice of impiety, and became temples of God.[^4972]
*Troparion 2:* From violent hands ye save all who are under your hand, O hierarchs, righteous ones, and venereables, preserving the flock in Christ; therefore are ye called blessed.[^4973]
*Troparion 3:* Following the prophetic sayings, the multitude of women, both in sufferings and in fasting, were well-pleasing unto God the Word Who shone forth from a Virgin woman.[^4974]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
O Life of all the dead, settle those who from among us have passed in faith unto Thee, the Creator, in the light with Thy saints, O Plenteous in mercy.[^4975]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
With mouth, and tongue, and heart do I confess thee, O Maiden, as the pure Mother of our God: therefore by thine intercession deliver me from eternal condemnation.[^4976]

*Another Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, cleansed from the gore of demons by the Blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.*[^4977]

*Troparion 1:* Having beheld the lance which pierced Thy divine side, O Savior, let the turning sword depart from Thy servants, by the supplications of Thy passion-bearers.[^4978]
*Troparion 2:* Suspended upon the Tree, O my Savior, Thou didst open Paradise: settle therein those who have been translated in faith, as Thou art compassionate, and reveal them as partakers of Thy life.[^4979]
*Glory...:* Vouchsafe those who piously pass over unto Thee, O Master, through death, to enjoy the pasture of life, and number them with the righteous from all ages.[^4980]
*Both now...:* The Word, being invisible God, became visible by being incarnate of the unwedded Virgin Maiden, and by His death He destroyed death.[^4981]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Abrahamic youths of old in Babylon trampled down the flame of the furnace, crying aloud in hymns: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*[^4982]

*Troparion 1:* As consecrated vessels of the Master Christ, ramparts and pillars of the Church, let us harmoniously hymn the Lord's martyrs in gladness of soul.[^4983]
*Troparion 2:* Christ's hierarchs and the divine choir of all the venereables rejoice with the hosts on high: by their prayers, O Christ, save those who hymn Thee.[^4984]
*Troparion 3:* The noble endurance of women who shone forth in monasticism and suffering, and who ascetically labored in faith, cast down him who through disobedience wounded Eve.[^4985]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
O Thou Who didst destroy hades and trample down death by Thy death, give rest unto those whom Thou hast translated in faith, and show them to be dwellers of Paradise.[^4986]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Beholding the flame which of old burned not the bush, the Lawgiver was instructed concerning the image of thy childbearing, O ever-blessed Virgin Birthgiver of God.[^4987]

*Another Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic youths, consumed more by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*[^4988]

*Troparion 1:* Accepting the endurance, and expectation, and the blood of all the martyrs as ransom, give rest with Thee unto those who have piously fallen asleep, as Thou art merciful and easy to be reconciled.[^4989]
*Troparion 2:* Having numbered with the firstborn and with Thy righteous ones, O Savior, the souls of Thy servants translated unto Thee,...[^4990]

...vouchsafe unceasingly to enjoy Thee, Who rulest over all.[^4991]
*Glory...:* With gladness, boldness, and radiance, O Word of God, our Redeemer, be well-pleased as God that those whom Thou hast now received as Thou art merciful may meet Thee in the clouds.[^4992]
*Both now...:* Blessed Virgin Birthgiver of God, Mother, rejoice! For for thy sake destruction of death truly came to pass, and indissoluble life was granted unto the dead.[^4993]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*O all-powerful Redeemer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew the godly youths and teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and praise the Lord!*[^4994]

*Troparion 1:* Hierarchs, prophets, and martyrs, having finished the sacred contest, obtained a sacred dwelling with the angels; with whom they ask purification and great mercy for us all.[^4995]
*Troparion 2:* Enlightened by the Spirit, the venereables destroyed the darkness of demons; with them the hieromartyrs and hierarchs, and prophets with the righteous, glorify God in praise.[^4996]
*Troparion 3:* He who formerly boasted in madness to destroy earth and sea is ever trampled down by women, who with zeal served God both in suffering and in fasting.[^4997]
*Glory... For the Departed:*
For all who have fallen asleep in faith, O Christ, we pray Thee: as Thou art compassionate, number them with the choirs of the saved, who unceasingly cry aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord![^4998]
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Vouchsafe me Thy mercy, O Lady Birthgiver of God, who didst bear the most merciful Word, and save me who cry aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord![^4999]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Daniel, stretching forth his hands, shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*[^5000]

*Troparion 1:* Receiving the supplications of the martyrs and showing mercy unto Thy kinsmen, give rest, O Master, unto the souls of those who have fallen asleep in Thy faith, overlooking their sins, who cry aloud unto Thee: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord![^5001]
*Troparion 2:* Accounted dead among two lawless men, Thou didst pour forth unto the dead...[^5002]

...indissoluble life; vouchsafe therefore Thy departed servants, who in hope of resurrection cry aloud unto Thee, to attain Thy Kingdom, O Savior: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^5003]

*Glory...*
O ever-flowing fountain of goodness, do Thou, O Savior, with gracious reconciliation receive into the heavenly mansions Thy departed servants who have left this corruptible life, as they cry aloud unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^5004]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou alone on earth hast appeared as an all-pure Virgin and unwedded Mother; for beyond word and understanding thou didst bear God, O Lady, and didst pour forth eternal life unto the dead; therefore we all bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God.[^5005]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve indeed, through the affliction of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the blossoming shoot of thy gestation, hast made blessing blossom forth unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.*[^5006]

*Troparion 1:* Beholding the divine gifts and receiving the rewards of their great sufferings, the martyrs rejoice, magnifying Christ, who hath truly exalted them and manifested them as victors.[^5007]

*Troparion 2:* Having been set as hierarchs to minister unto the people, O God-proclaiming hierarchs; and having been enlightened by fasting, O venereables, ye shone forth brighter than the sun, illuminating the faithful by the manifestation of great deeds.[^5008]

*Troparion 3:* Together with the hieromartyrs and with all the prophets, let us bless all the venereables and righteous ones, and the women who strove brightly, crying aloud: By their supplications, O Christ, deliver our souls from Gehenna.[^5009]

*Glory... For the Departed:*
Vouchsafe those who have passed over unto Thee in faith, O Christ, to partake of that joy whereof the saints were accounted worthy who well pleased Thee, overlooking their transgressions, O Lord, who alone art plenteous in mercy.[^5010]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having appeared higher than the cherubim on high, O All-Immaculate One, as having borne the Sustainer of all things, make my mind higher than earthly things, strengthening me against carnal passions to do the desires of the Master.[^5011]

*Another Canon, Same Heirmos:*
*Troparion 1:* Thou hast given boldness unto the true sufferers and martyrs to pray unto Thee, O Lord; grant...[^5012]

...also unto those who in faith have passed over, divine deliverance for their sake, granting them to dwell in the place of holy habitation.[^5013]

*Troparion 2:* By Thy all-creating beckoning, Thou who workest all things for good, possessing dominion over the living and ruling over the dead as Thou art all-powerful: place Thy servants whom Thou hast translated beside the waters of rest.[^5014]

*Glory...*
O Thou who by nature art good, who art rich in mercies and in loving-kindness, deliver from outer darkness those who call upon Thy Name; and having justified them by faith and grace, enlighten them, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^5015]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The prophets proclaimed the prefigurations of thy giving birth, O All-Immaculate One, and others in diverse manners composed most glorious titles for thee; for thou didst bear Life unto those in hades, destroying the dominion of death.[^5016]

*It is truly meet, and a prostration; the litany, the Exapostilarion, and the customary Psalms.*[^5017]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 4:*
Partakers with the angels have ye become, O holy martyrs, having manfully confessed Christ before the judgment seat; for all the fair things in the world ye abandoned as non-existent, and held fast unto faith as a steadfast hope. Therefore, having driven away delusion, ye pour forth gifts of healing unto the faithful, unceasingly praying that our souls be saved.[^5018]

How should we not marvel at your struggles, O holy martyrs? For being clothed with a mortal body, ye conquered bodiless foes; the torturers' threats did not terrify you. Truly worthily were ye glorified by Christ.[^5019]

Precious is the death of Thy saints, O Lord! For by sword and fire, and with a contrite soul, they poured out their blood, having hope in Thee to receive the reward of their labors; for they endured and received from Thee, O Savior, great mercy.[^5020]

*For the Departed:*
In Thy resting-place, O Lord, where all Thy saints find rest, give rest unto Thy departed servants, as Thou alone art the Lover of mankind.[^5021]

*Glory...*
Where is attachment to the world? Where is transient fantasy? Where is gold and silver? Where...[^5022]

...is the multitude of servants and clamor? All is dust, all is ashes, all is shadow! But come, let us cry aloud unto the immortal King: O Lord, vouchsafe those who have passed over from us Thy eternal blessings, and give them rest in unfading blessedness.[^5023]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O only pure and immaculate Virgin, who without seed didst bear God: entreat Him that our souls be saved.[^5024]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Truly dread is the mystery of death! How is the soul violently severed from the body? From its joints and the harmony of the natural union it is divided by the divine will. Therefore we pray Thee: Give rest unto the departed in the tabernacles of Thy righteous ones, O Life-giver and Lover of mankind.[^5025]

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord.[^5026]

Death appears as sleep unto those who believe in Thee, since Thou who rulest over all wast laid in the tomb, and didst destroy the dominion of death, bringing to naught its ancient tyranny. Therefore we pray Thee: Place the departed in the joy of Thy saints and in the radiance of the righteous.[^5027]

*Verse:* Their souls shall dwell amid good things.[^5028]

Thou hast become for us righteousness and sanctification, and redemption for our souls; for Thou hast brought us unto the Father, justified and redeemed, having taken upon Thyself the penalty due unto us; and now we pray Thee: Give rest unto the departed in the joy and radiance of Thy saints, O Benefactor and Lover of mankind.[^5029]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
Following the words of the divinely speaking prophets, we confess thee to be the Theotokos, O All-Immaculate One; for thou didst bear God ineffably incarnate, who hath delivered us who were held captive by sins. Him do thou now entreat, O Most Pure One, to enlighten with His radiance His departed servants.[^5030]

*It is good to give praise: Trisagion. After Our Father: Troparion, Litany, and the First Hour.*[^5031]

*On Saturday, at the Beatitudes, Tone 4:*
*Troparion 1:* Through the tree Adam was banished from Paradise, but through the tree of the Cross the thief was made to dwell in Paradise; for the one by tasting set at naught the commandment...[^5032]

...of the Creator, while the other, being crucified together with Thee, confessed God hidden in the flesh, crying aloud: Remember me in Thy Kingdom![^5033]

*Troparion 2:* Being inflamed with the love of Christ, O holy martyrs, ye quenched the fire of impiety by the dew of your struggles, and were manifested as most radiant luminaries of the Church, driving away the darkness of diseases and afflictions from our souls by goodness; therefore we worthily praise you.[^5034]

*Troparion 3:* The holy hierarchs, and the divine company of the prophets, and the choirs of the venereables, and the unique triumph of holy women, having well pleased God by virtuous deeds, were glorified. We call them blessed, praying that through their supplications we may obtain eternal life and enlightenment.[^5035]

*Troparion 4:* Where Thy light shines, where the choirs of the saints rejoice, whence sighing and sorrow have fled away: place Thy servants who have fallen asleep beforehand and left this life of many pains, to hymn Thine ineffable goodness, O Christ, overlooking their transgressions which they committed on earth.[^5036]

*Glory... Trinity:*
O indivisible Trinity, united in all things in Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, unconfused in personal properties, O tri-hypostatic Unity: give rest unto those who in faith have died unto Thee, through the supplications of Thy holy martyrs, of the fathers, and of the honorable prophets, granting cleansing unto our souls.[^5037]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Word, whom the Father begat before the morning star, thou didst conceive, and didst bear in the flesh as perfect Man, made known in two energies and two wills, O Most Pure Maiden and Bride of God: entreat Him, as Creator and Lord, to show mercy unto us who hymn thee.[^5038]

*End of Tone 4.*[^5039]

---

[^4188]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1 of the Penitential Canon, praying against public exposure at the Dread Judgment.
[^4189]: Troparion 2 of Ode 1.

[^4191]: Martyricon 2 of Ode 1, enumerating the physical tortures endured by the martyrs.

[^4193]: Rubric for the second Canon at Tuesday Matins: To the Holy Forerunner John (Дрꙋгі́й Канѡ́нъ, ст҃о́мꙋ І҆ѡа́ннꙋ Предте́чи).
[^4194]: Acrostic and authorship rubric for the Canon to the Forerunner: "A prayerful song I weave for thee with love, O blessed one" (Пѣ́снь моли́твеннꙋю плетꙋ̀ тебѣ̀ Блаже́нне любо́вїю), by Joseph the Hymnographer (Ὠιδὴν λιταίαν σοι πλέκω μάκαρ πόθῳ).
[^4195]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon to the Forerunner (Трїста́ты крѣ̑пкїѧ), referencing Exodus 15:4 ("his chosen captains also are drowned in the Red sea") and interpreting the tripartite division of the soul (rational, incensive, appetitive—τὸ τριμερὲς τῆς ψυχῆς) in the depth of dispassion (ἀπάθεια).
[^4196]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Forerunner, comparing St. John to the Morning Star (Phosphoros/Eosphoros) preceding the Sun of Righteousness.
[^4197]: Conclusion of Troparion 2 of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Forerunner, continuing from physical leaf p429: "...show forth through thy prayers to be fruitful, bringing forth the goodly offspring of virtues" (покажѝ моли́твами твои́ми плоди́тельнꙋ, добродѣ́телей благоча́дїе приносѧ́щꙋю).
[^4198]: Troparion 3 of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Forerunner, referencing Luke 1:17 and Malachi 4:5 on John coming in the spirit and power of Elijah.
[^4199]: Theotokion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Forerunner, invoking Mary as the radiant cloud (Isaiah 19:1) and alluding to Psalm 35:9 [LXX Psalm 35:10]: "in Thy light shall we see light".
[^4200]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Penitential Canon (Оу҆твержда́ѧй гро́мъ, и҆ созида́ѧй дꙋ́хъ), quoting Amos 4:13 LXX: "For, behold, I am he that strengthens the thunder, and creates the wind, and proclaims to men his Christ" (διότι ἰδοὺ ἐγὼ στερεῶν βροντὴν καὶ κτίζων πνεῦμα), and 1 Kings 2:2 [1 Samuel 2:2 LXX]: "there is none holy as the Lord".
[^4201]: Troparion 1 of Ode 3 of the Penitential Canon, referencing Christ healing the blind (John 9) as a model of spiritual illumination.
[^4202]: Troparion 2 of Ode 3, echoing the Lenten hymn *The doors of repentance open unto me, O Giver of Life* (Покая́нїя отве́рзи ми́ две́ри, Жизнода́вче).
[^4203]: Martyricon 1 of Ode 3, commemorating the overthrow of Beliar (2 Corinthians 6:15) through the sufferings of the martyrs.
[^4204]: Martyricon 2 of Ode 3, using "noetic Ethiopians" (мы́сленныхъ мꙋ́ринѡвъ) in the traditional Patristic and ascetic sense to designate demonic powers of spiritual darkness (e.g. Life of St. Anthony, Apophthegmata Patrum).
[^4205]: Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Penitential Canon, celebrating the reversal of the ancestral grief of Adam and Eve.
[^4206]: Rubric for the second Canon: Ode 3, the Heirmos (И҆́нъ, І҆рмо́съ).
[^4207]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon to the Forerunner (Лꙋ́къ си́льныхъ и҆знемо́же), based on the Song of Hannah in 1 Kings 2:4 [1 Samuel 2:4 LXX].
[^4208]: Troparion 1 of Ode 3 of the Canon to the Forerunner, praising St. John's ascetic life and preaching of repentance (Matthew 3:1-8).
[^4209]: Troparion 2 of Ode 3 of the Canon to the Forerunner, petitioning deliverance from the "irrational passions" (безслове́сныхъ страсте́й) and guidance toward holy dispassion (ἀπάθεια).
[^4210]: CSD: *житїѐ Безпло́тныхъ показа́лъ є҆сѝ* ("thou didst show forth the life of the Bodiless Powers"). The ascetic life of the Forerunner in the Judean wilderness is traditionally regarded as the earthly exemplar of angelic, bodiless contemplation.
[^4211]: CSD: *престꙋпле́нїемъ непотре́бенъ бы́сть* ("had become unprofitable through transgression"), echoing LXX Ps. 13:3 / Rom. 3:12 (*πάντες ἐξέκλιναν, ἅμα ἠχρειώθησαν*).
[^4212]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the first penitential canon, Tone 4. Scripture: Canticle of Habakkuk (LXX Hab. 3:2-3).
[^4213]: CSD: *Ѡ҆бнаже́нъ бы́хъ добродѣ́телей* ("I have been stripped of virtues"). Patristic metaphor of sin as the stripping of the original luminous garment and clothing oneself in the darkness of malice.
[^4214]: CSD: *Препла́ваѧ мірска́гѡ мо́рѧ Сло́ве во́дꙋ* ("Sailing across the water of the sea of this world, O Word"). Common hymnographic maritime metaphor: the storm of worldly cares and bodily passions contrasted with the haven of repentance (*покаѧ́нїѧ приста́нище*).
[^4215]: CSD: *гно́й ве́сь грѣха̀* ("all the corruption/putrefaction of sin").
[^4216]: CSD: *Вала́еми по землѝ ꙗ҆́кѡже ка́менїе* ("Being rolled upon the ground like stones", Greek: κυλινδούμενοι ὡς λίθοι). The bodily dragging and crushing of martyrs over rough stones.
[^4217]: Theotokion petition for uninterrupted salvation across every temporal and spatial circumstance (*во всѧ́комъ вре́мени и҆ мѣ́стѣ*).
[^4218]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the second canon (to the Holy Forerunner), Tone 4. Scripture: Habakkuk 3.
[^4219]: Typological imagery: the Forerunner as the mystical turtledove (*го́рлица*) announcing the spiritual spring (*ве́сна и҆́стиннаѧ*), Christ (cf. Cant. 2:12).
[^4220]: CSD: *ве́тхомꙋ и҆ но́вомꙋ ꙗ҆́кѡ сы́й хода́тай* ("as thou art the mediator of the Old and the New"). St. John the Baptist as the prophetic boundary connecting the Old Covenant dispensation to the revelation of the New Covenant in Christ.
[^4221]: Contrast between the holy wilderness (*пꙋсты́нѧ*) where the Baptist dwelt blamelessly and the desolate wilderness of the sinful mind (*ѡ҆пꙋстѣ́вшїй оу҆́мъ*).
[^4222]: Theotokion of Ode 4 of the second canon: Christ recognized as purification and redemption (*Ѡ҆чище́нїе на́мъ и҆ и҆збавле́нїе*).
[^4223]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the first penitential canon, Tone 4. Scripture: Canticle of Isaiah (LXX Isa. 26:9: *Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει τὸ πνεῦμά μου πρὸς σέ, ὁ Θεός, διότι φῶς τὰ προστάγματά σου ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς*).
[^4224]: Deliverance from the demon of despondency (*оу҆ны́нїе*, Greek: ἀκηδία) and diabolic delusion.
[^4225]: Spiritual pride and fall: the heart exalted by serpentine suggestion and plummeting into great catastrophe (*вознесе́сѧ се́рдце моѐ прило́гми ѕмі́ѧ, и҆ низпаде́сѧ паде́нїемъ ве́лїимъ*, cf. Prov. 16:18).
[^4226]: Quenching the fiery furnace of pagan polytheism with streams of martyric blood (*ка́плями крове́й*).
[^4227]: Specific martyric tortures: scraping with iron claws (*ногтьмѝ стро́жеми*) and decollation/beheading (*въ главꙋ̀ оу҆сѣка́еми*).
[^4228]: Theotokion petition: the dawn of the Virgin's mercy (*зарю̀ ми́лости*) guiding the soul out of darkness into the light of repentance.
[^4229]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the second canon, Tone 4.
[^4230]: Typological juxtaposition: wilderness burning coals of passions assuaged by the dewy moisture of the Baptist's prayers (*росо́ю твои́хъ моли́твъ*).
[^4231]: CSD: *Ѿ твоеѧ̀ Всеблаже́нне ст҃ы́ѧ десни́цы, креща́етсѧ Оу҆ц҃а̀ бж҃твеннаѧ десни́ца* ("By thy holy right hand, O all-blessed one, the divine right hand of the Father is baptized"). Dramatic theological paradox: Christ as the Father's right hand baptized by the hand of His servant.
[^4232]: Universal intercession of St. John the Baptist: refuge, sovereign protection, and great wall (*прибѣ́жище, покро́въ держа́венъ, и҆ вели́каѧ стѣна̀*).
[^4233]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 46:5 (*τὴν καλλονὴν Ἰακώβ, ἣν ἠγάπησεν*). The Virgin Mary typified as the divine beauty of Jacob.
[^4234]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the first penitential canon, Tone 4. Scripture: Canticle of Jonah (LXX Jonah 2:3-7).
[^4235]: CSD: *Дрема́нїемъ небреже́нїѧ ѡ҆тѧготи́хъ ѻ҆каѧ́нный дꙋ́шꙋ* ("With the slumber of negligence I, wretched one, have weighed down my soul").
[^4236]: Penitential self-examination before the inevitable terror of the future judgment-seat (*стра́шнагѡ сꙋди́ща ѻ҆́нагѡ*).
[^4237]: CSD: *Цѣвни́ца* ("harp/pipe/lyre", Greek: κινύра). The martyrs as musical instruments of the Holy Spirit.
[^4238]: Patristic martyric motif: the martyrs triumphing over fleshly torments as though suffering in bodies not their own (*ꙗ҆́кѡ въ тѣлесѣ́хъ чꙋжди́хъ*).
[^4239]: Theotokion petition for maternal assistance at the dread judgment hour (*въ ча́съ сꙋ́дный*).
[^4240]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the second canon, Tone 4. Scripture: LXX Ps. 68:3, 15.
[^4241]: The Baptist standing in the Jordan waters (*въ быстри́нахъ*) baptizing the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world (John 1:29).
[^4242]: CSD: *возникнове́нїа не и҆мꙋ́щее* ("having no upward look/gaze", Greek: ἀνανεῦσαι μὴ ἔχουσαν). The soul paralyzed by shame and earthbound sins, unable to look up to heaven.
[^4243]: St. John the Baptist crying out in the uninhabited desert to prepare the way of the Lord (Matt. 3:3, Mark 1:3).
[^4244]: The fulfillment (*событїѐ*) of Old Testament shadows and types (*Зако́на ѻ҆́брази*) in the virginal conception of the Word.
[^4245]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the first penitential canon, Tone 4. Scripture: The Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (LXX Dan. 3:26ff.).
[^4246]: Incarnation theology: Christ appearing as a newborn child (*ю҆́но ѻ҆троча̀*) to renew humanity grown old in transgression.
[^4247]: Triad of biblical repentance: King Manasseh (Prayer of Manasseh), the weeping sinful woman (Luke 7:37-50), and the penitent thief (Luke 23:40-43).
[^4248]: The ship of Christian piety steered by Christ across the tempest of polytheistic idolatry (*ѡ҆кормле́нїемъ Хрⷭ҇то́вымъ*).
[^4249]: CSD: *запеча́танїа честна̑ѧ страсте́й Хрⷭ҇то́выхъ* ("honorable seals of the sufferings of Christ"). The martyrs' wounds bearing the authentic impression of the Passion of the Lord.
[^4250]: Theotokion petition: glorifying the transcendent compassions of God made manifest through Christ.
[^4251]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the second canon, Tone 4.
[^4252]: St. John the Baptist as the greatest of all born of women (Matt. 11:11), entreating deliverance from the fiery flame and outer darkness.
[^4253]: Evangelical parable: the barren fig tree threatened with the axe (Matt. 3:10, 21:19; Luke 13:6-9).
[^4254]: CSD: *бо́дренными твои́ми моли́твами* ("by thy vigilant prayers").
[^4255]: Theotokion of Ode 7 of the second canon: deliverance from demonic servitude and the lot of Satan.
[^4256]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the first penitential canon, Tone 4. Scripture: Canticle of the Three Youths (Dan. 3:57ff.).
[^4257]: Confession of transgressions and disobedience to divine commandments, pleading for gratuitous mercy (*тꙋ́не*, without merit).
[^4258]: Spiritual husbandry: the sickle of the fear of God cutting down the thorns of passion, allowing the fruitful ear of repentance (*спаси́тельный кла́съ*) to sprout.
[^4259]: Antinomy of martyric triumph: bodily constriction and torment opening into spacious spiritual freedom through divine grace (*оу҆тѣснѧ́еми ... разпространи́шасѧ*).
[^4260]: The deceiver (*льсти́вый*) brought down into the abyss of defeat through the patient endurance of the holy martyrs.
[^4261]: Canticles metaphor: the womb of the Mother of God likened to a heap of wheat upon the threshing-floor (Cant. 7:2), nurturing the world with the bread of life.
[^4262]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the second canon, Tone 4.
[^4263]: Scripture: Rom. 13:13 (*ὡς ἐν ἡμέρᾳ εὐσχημόνως περιπατήσωμεν*, "let us walk becomingly, as in the day").
[^4264]: The ship of the soul tossed upon the billows of temptation, guided to port through the Baptist's prayers.
[^4265]: The streams of St. John's prayers drying up the fathomless ocean of sins.
[^4266]: Theophany witness: the voice of the Father and the descent of the Holy Spirit at the baptism of Jesus (Matt. 3:16-17).
[^4267]: The Theotokos as the wellspring of human recreation and renewal (*и҆сто́чникъ на́шегѡ ѡ҆бновле́нїа*).
[^4268]: Slavonic rubrical call for the Magnificat (LXX Luke 1:46-55: *Вели́читъ дꙋша̀ моѧ̀ Гдⷭ҇а*).
[^4269]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the first penitential canon, Tone 4. Scripture: Conflation of the Magnificat (LXX Luke 1:51-52) and the Benedictus (LXX Luke 1:78-79).
[^4270]: Parable of the Wise and Foolish Virgins (Matt. 25:1-13): entering the mystical bridal chamber with lamps illuminated by the oil of virtues.
[^4271]: The penitent woman weeping at the feet of Christ (Luke 7:38, 50: *Ἡ πίστις σου σέσωκέν σε*).
[^4272]: The scars of the martyrs shining as the ornament of beauty and dignity in heaven (cf. Gal. 6:17).
[^4273]: The holy relics of the martyrs acting as an ark of divine defense (*дрꙋгі́й ковче́гъ*).
[^4274]: CSD: *Благолюби́ваѧ Ѻ҆трокови́це* ("Goodness-loving Maiden", Greek: φιλάγαθε).
[^4275]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the second canon, Tone 4. Patristic typology: Eve's disobedience bringing the curse into the world contrasted with Mary's virginal childbearing bringing forth divine blessing.
[^4276]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 117:14 (*Ἰσχύς μου καὶ ὕμνησίς μου ὁ Κύριος*). Christ as the strength and song of the Christian soul.
[^4277]: Prophetic imagery: St. John the Baptist as the turtledove and swallow announcing the arrival of Christ, the true Spring.
[^4278]: St. John leaping for joy in Elizabeth's womb at the approach of the Virgin Mary bearing the incarnate Word (Luke 1:41, 44).
[^4279]: Scripture: James 2:13 (*Ἡ γὰρ κρίσις ἀνέλεος τῷ μὴ ποιήσαντι ἔλεος*, "judgment without mercy unto him that hath showed no mercy") and the midnight arrival of the Bridegroom (Matt. 25:6).
[^4280]: Theotokion of Ode 9 of the second canon: entreating the Mother of God to inspire love for divine goodness and hatred of fleshly lust.
[^4281]: Matins Aposticha stichera designated in Ruthenian Octoechos usage as penitential sessional hymns (*сѣда́лны покаѧ́нны*).
[^4282]: Sessional hymn / Apostichon: the lost sheep crying out unto the Good Shepherd (LXX Ps. 118:176, Luke 15:4-7, John 10:11).
[^4283]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 89:14, 16.
[^4284]: Invocation of Peter sinking in the sea of Galilee (Matt. 14:30-31).
[^4285]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 89:17.
[^4286]: Martyricon sticheron: communion of the martyrs with the angelic host and their unceasing intercession for healing and salvation.
[^4287]: Concluding Theotokion of the Matins Aposticha.
[^4288]: Daily Matins conclusion rubrics: LXX Ps. 91:2 (*It is good to give praise to the Lord*), followed by the Trisagion prayers through the Lord's Prayer.
[^4289]: The Divine Liturgy cycle for Tuesday.
[^4290]: The Beatitudes (*Блаже́нна*) stichera, Tone 4.
[^4291]: The standard Tone 4 Beatitudes heirmologic model: Adam's expulsion by the tree of knowledge contrasted with the good thief's entry into Paradise by the tree of the Cross (Luke 23:42).
[^4292]: Penitential triad: the tears of Peter (Matt. 26:75), the tears of the harlot (Luke 7:38), and the sighing of the publican (Luke 18:13).
[^4293]: St. John the Baptist as the loosener of barrenness, invoked to loosen spiritual infertility.
[^4294]: Enumeration of the physical trials of the martyrs: wild beasts, sword, drowning, fire, and iron instruments.
[^4295]: Triadikon of the Beatitudes: orthodox confession of the Holy Trinity (one divine essence in three distinct, unconfused Hypostases).
[^4296]: Theotokion of the Beatitudes: the virginal conception of the co-eternal and co-enthroned Son of God.
[^4297]: Opening of Wednesday liturgical services: in Byzantine-Ruthenian liturgical theology, Wednesday is dedicated to the Precious and Life-Giving Cross and the Passion of Christ, beginning with Tuesday evening Vespers.
[^4298]: Tuesday Evening Vespers rubric (*Въ Вто́рникъ ве́чера*).
[^4299]: Melodic model: *Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ)*, corresponding to Byzantine Greek: Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν Μάρτυσιν.
[^4300]: Colossians 2:14: Christ signing the charter of human liberation with His own blood and cancelling the bond of debt that stood against us.
[^4301]: Enumeration of the instruments of the Passion (*arma Christi*): Cross, spear, reed, sponge, nails, scourgings, purple robe, crown of thorns, spittings, and mockings.
[^4302]: Veneration and kissing of the Precious Cross (*Крестꙋ̀ твое́мꙋ честно́мꙋ покланѧ́юсѧ*).
[^4303]: Stavrotheotokia stichera to the Mother of God at the Cross, Tone 4, following the same automelon model.
[^4304]: Stavrotheotokion: the Mother of God as the Ewe-lamb (*А҆́гница*) lamenting at the foot of the Cross over the ungrateful Jewish assembly.
[^4305]: The Virgin's maternal lamentation over Christ the Lamb and Shepherd (John 1:29, 10:11) nailed upon the tree.
[^4306]: The Mother of God entreating the Lord not to leave her orphaned and alone in the world, but to hasten His Resurrection.
[^4307]: Stavrotheotokion at *Glory... Both now...*, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ*).
[^4308]: The maternal grief and contemplation of the voluntary Passion of Christ (*твое́мꙋ во́льномꙋ распѧ́тїю*).
[^4309]: Tuesday Vespers Aposticha stichera of the Precious Cross.
[^4310]: The Cross as an invincible weapon against demonic assaults (*Ѡ҆рꙋ́жїе непобѣди́мое*).
[^4311]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 122:1-2 (*Πρὸς σὲ ἦρα τοὺς ὀφθαλμούς μου*).
[^4312]: Trampling underfoot the snares of the adversary through the power of the Cross.
[^4313]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 122:3-4 (*Ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς, Κύριε, ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς*).
[^4314]: Martyricon sticheron: the boldness of the holy martyrs interceding for the remission of sins.
[^4315]: Melodic model: *Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше)*, corresponding to Greek: Ὁ ἐξ ὑψίστου κληθείς.
[^4316]: Stavrotheotokion: Christ consoling His Mother from the Cross, promising His impending Resurrection and the harrowing of Hades (theological antitype of Ode 9 of Holy Saturday Matins: *Не рыда́й менѐ Ма́ти*).
[^4317]: Tuesday Compline office. Codicological note: 1895 Lviv Stauropegion print contains the typesetter typographical error *Въ Вто́рникъ на ПОКЕЧЕ́РЇИ* (sic, with 'К' for 'В').
[^4318]: Ode 1 Heirmos of Tuesday Compline Canon, Tone 4 (*Ѡ҆тве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моѧ̑*, Greek: Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου). One of the most famous heirmoi in the Byzantine rite, also sung at the Akathist Hymn.
[^4319]: Canon refrain: *Прест҃а́ѧ Гпⷭ҇же Бцⷣе, спасѝ на́съ* ("Most-holy Lady Theotokos, save us").
[^4320]: Incipit of Troparion 1 of Ode 1 of Compline Canon (*Неизмѣ́рнꙋю Бж҃їю мꙋ́дрость и҆ си́лꙋ ро́ждши Пренепоро́чнаѧ*), concluding physical leaf p440 and continuing onto physical leaf p441 in Cohort 45.
[^4321]: Compline Canon to the Most-holy Theotokos, Tone 4, Ode 1 Troparion 1 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p440 in Cohort 44): Christ as the power and dominion of strength born without seed of the Virgin.
[^4322]: Ode 1 Troparion 2: the Virgin's childbearing beyond understanding, enriching earthborn mankind with incorruption as the Mediatress of renewal (*хода́таицꙋ ѡ҆бновле́нїѧ*).
[^4323]: Ode 1, Glory: petition for healing of soul and body through the overshadowing of the Mother of God, the protectress of those in sorrow.
[^4324]: Ode 1, Both now: prayer for the hand of salvation to be stretched out amid the tempest of tribulations, delivering the soul from the deep of evils.
[^4325]: Ode 3 Heirmos (*Твоѧ̑ пѣсносло́вцы Бцⷣе*, Greek: Τοὺς σοὺς ὑμνολόγους, Θεοτόκε). Classical Byzantine heirmos of the Theotokos attributed to St. John of Damascus, praying for crowns of glory.
[^4326]: Ode 3 Troparion 1: the Mother of God as a fountain of tender-heartedness sprinkling streams of healing upon the sick.
[^4327]: Ode 3 Troparion 2: the Virgin as a fair bridal-chamber (*красе́нъ черто́гъ*) and bridal room beyond nature for the divine Word.
[^4328]: Ode 3, Glory: confession of soul-crushing despondency and prayer unto the Lady who bore Life to grant consolation.
[^4329]: Ode 3, Both now: supplication for mercy and cleansing before the hour of death, delivered from crafty demons.
[^4330]: Ode 4 Heirmos (*Неизслѣ́дный Бж҃їй совѣ́тъ*, Greek: Τὴν ἀνεξιχνίαστον θείαν βουλήν). Typological ode based on the Canticle of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:2), beholding the unsearchable mystery of the Incarnation.
[^4331]: Ode 4 Troparion 1: the King of all choosing the Virgin from the royal root and loins of David, exalting her above the Cherubim and Seraphim.
[^4332]: Ode 4 Troparion 2 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p441): the Creator becoming incarnate and showing forth the Virgin as the unerring protectress of Christians.
[^4333]: Ode 4, Glory: the Virgin invoked as protection, defense, and refuge against immeasurable evils.
[^4334]: Ode 4, Both now: prayer for tears, groaning, and deliverance from impassioned thoughts unto divine compunction.
[^4335]: Ode 5 Heirmos (*Оу҆жасо́шасѧ всѧ́ческаѧ*, Greek: Ἐξέστη τὰ σύμπанта). The cosmic wonder of the unwedded Virgin carrying the God over all in her womb.
[^4336]: Ode 5 Troparion 1: the Virgin as an animate cloud (*ѡ҆́блакъ ѡ҆дꙋшевле́нъ*) of Christ the King, showering the dew of healing upon the sick.
[^4337]: Ode 5 Troparion 2: prayer to the Master for release from sorrows and forgiveness of transgressions, leading unto incorruptible joy.
[^4338]: Ode 5, Glory: the Theotokos as the hope and boast of salvation, anticipating and delivering the soul swallowed by sins.
[^4339]: Ode 5, Both now: Stavrotheotokion; Christ as the ripe cluster of grapes (*Гро́здъ зрѣ́лый*, cf. Num. 13:23) hung upon the Tree, and the Sun of glory darkening creation in His Passion.
[^4340]: Ode 6 Heirmos (*Прїидо́хъ въ глꙋбины̀ морскї́ѧ*, Greek: Ἦλθον εἰς τὰ βάθη τῆς θαλάσσης). Jonas-canticle typifying deliverance from the abyss of sins (LXX Ps. 68:3, Jon. 2:3-6).
[^4341]: Ode 6 Troparion 1: Christ taking the form of a servant (Phil. 2:7) and showing forth the Theotokos higher than the Cherubim and dread Seraphim.
[^4342]: Ode 6 Troparion 2: the Virgin bearing divine Life and cutting down the insurrection of passions.
[^4343]: Ode 6, Glory: petition for deliverance from perils and transgressions through the Mediatress who bore the Redemption of all.
[^4344]: Ode 6, Both now: the faithful boasting in the Virgin and remaining unafraid of barbarian invasions.
[^4345]: Rubric: Trisagion prayers, Lord have mercy thrice, Glory... Both now... and concluding sessional hymn.
[^4346]: Sessional Hymn at Compline, Tone 4 (*Дѣ́во пренепоро́чнаѧ, Ма́ти Хрⷭ҇та̀ Бг҃а*): Simeon's prophecy fulfilled at the voluntary Crucifixion (Lk. 2:35).
[^4347]: Ode 7 Heirmos (*Не послꙋжи́ша тва́ри бг҃омꙋ́дрїи*, Greek: Οὐκ ἐλάτρευσαν τῇ κτίσει οἱ θεόφρονες). Canticle of the Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian furnace (Dan. 3:28).
[^4348]: Ode 7 Troparion 1: soul strength dried up like a potsherd (LXX Ps. 21:16) and drawing near to the destructive bonds of Hades.
[^4349]: Ode 7 Troparion 2: heart rent by manifold evils and sicknesses, delivered by the Mother of Life.
[^4350]: Ode 7, Glory: opening the celestial gates of the kingdom of Christ the all-merciful King.
[^4351]: Ode 7, Both now: the aggravated wounds of sin leading to spiritual and bodily death, averted by the Lady's might.
[^4352]: Ode 8 Heirmos (*Ѻ҆́троки благочести̑выѧ въ пещѝ*, Greek: Παῖδας εὐαγεῖς ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ). The furnace prefiguring the virgin birth that preserves without burning (Dan. 3:57).
[^4353]: Ode 8 Troparion 1 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p443): the furnace of thorn-bearing passions quenched and transgressions forgiven.
[^4354]: Ode 8 Troparion 2: the Virgin as the lampstand (*Свѣ́щникъ*) of the thrice-radiant Light dispelling the darkness of sins.
[^4355]: Ode 8, Glory: the transcendent God wholly dwelling in the womb of the Ever-virgin for universal salvation.
[^4356]: Ode 8, Both now: eschatological defense at the hour of death against demonic examination, toll-houses (*мыта́рства*), and the cruel prince.
[^4357]: Ode 9 Heirmos (*Всѧ́къ земноро́дный да взыгра́етсѧ*, Greek: Ἅπας γηγενής). Celebrated Megalynarion heirmos of the Theotokos inviting earthly mortals and angelic powers to keep festival.
[^4358]: Ode 9 Troparion 1: humanity exalted to divine sonship and adoption through the Incarnation of the Word.
[^4359]: Ode 9 Troparion 2: soul bound by the wounds of sin finding refuge in the protection of the Sovereign Lady.
[^4360]: Ode 9, Glory: poetic offering woven in compunction from a suffering soul to the Mother of God.
[^4361]: Ode 9, Both now: spiritual enlightenment of the inner eyes against the dense darkness of sin and the pit of despair.
[^4362]: Rubric: concluding dismissal rite of Compline (It is truly meet, prostration, Trisagion, and dismissal).
[^4363]: Wednesday Matins office of Tone 4. Rubric for Sessional Hymns of the Precious Cross following the first stichology of the Kathisma.
[^4364]: First Sessional Hymn of the Cross, Tone 4 (*И҆скꙋпи́лъ ны̀ є҆сѝ ѿ кла́твы зако́нныѧ*): Christ redeeming mankind from the curse of the law by His Blood (Gal. 3:13).
[^4365]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 98:5 (*Ὑψοῦτε Κύριον τὸν Θεὸν ἡμῶν*).
[^4366]: Second Sessional Hymn of the Cross: Christ crucified by the Jews to call the Gentiles into the kingdom.
[^4367]: Stavrotheotokion (*Безневѣ́стнаѧ Ма́ти твоѧ̀ Хрⷭ҇тѐ*): maternal lamentation of the Mother of God beholding her Son nailed to the Tree.
[^4368]: Sessional Hymns following the second stichology of the Kathisma at Wednesday Matins.
[^4369]: Sessional Hymn (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ*): petition for swift divine help against threatening foes, manifesting the power of the Orthodox faith.
[^4370]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 73:12 (*Ὁ δὲ Θεὸς βασιлеὺς ἡμῶν πρὸ αἰώνων*).
[^4371]: Sessional Hymn of the Cross: healing the primordial wound of Adam through Christ's voluntary Crucifixion on Golgotha.
[^4372]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 67:36 (*Θαυμαστὸς ὁ Θεὸς ἐν τοῖς ἁγίοις αὐτοῦ*).
[^4373]: Martyricon sessional hymn conclusion (continued from physical leaf p445): holy passion-bearers sanctifying the faithful through their endurance and intercession.
[^4374]: Stavrotheotokion: the immaculate Ewe-lamb wounded with sorrow at the Crucifixion of the Lord of life.
[^4375]: Sessional Hymns following the third stichology of the Kathisma at Wednesday Matins.
[^4376]: Sessional Hymn of the Cross: Christ raising fallen man upon Golgotha and destroying the deceitful serpent through the Tree.
[^4377]: Sessional Hymn of the Cross: cosmic upheavals, eclipse of the sun, and the resurrection of the dead at the Lord's Crucifixion (Mt. 27:45, 52).
[^4378]: Stavrotheotokion: Simeon's prophecy fulfilled at Golgotha (Lk. 2:35).
[^4379]: Matins Canon to the Cross and the Martyrs, acrostic: *The Cross is a weapon unto salvation* (Greek: Σταυρός ἐστιν ὅπλον εἰς σωτηρίαν), composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer.
[^4380]: Ode 1 Heirmos (*Мѡ́рѧ чермнꙋ́ю пꙋчи́нꙋ*, Greek: Θαλάσσης τὸ ἐρυθραῖον πέλαγος). Crossing the Red Sea and Moses' cruciform hands defeating Amalek (Ex. 14:21-29, 17:11-12).
[^4381]: Refrain to the Precious Cross: *Сла́ва Гдⷭ҇и крестꙋ̀ твое́мꙋ и҆ распѧ́тїю* ("Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross and crucifixion").
[^4382]: Ode 1 Troparion 1: Christ spreading out His hands upon the Cross to embrace all nations.
[^4383]: Ode 1 Troparion 2: the Cross protecting the believer against the spiritual wolf that seeks the soul's destruction.
[^4384]: Ode 1 Martyricon 1 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p446): the holy martyrs inheriting a life free of pain and relieving our sicknesses.
[^4385]: Ode 1 Martyricon 2: the martyrs binding the deceiver through their voluntary bonds in Christ.
[^4386]: Ode 1 Theotokion: perpetual virginity preserved in giving birth to God Who was lifted up upon the Cross.
