[^4389]: Ode 1 Troparion 2: deliverance from future weeping and lamentation at the dread judgment of God.
[^4390]: Ode 1 Troparion 3: freedom from the primordial curse and carnal slavery through the Virgin's intercession.
[^4391]: Ode 3 Heirmos (*Весели́тсѧ ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ це́рковь твоѧ̀ Хрⷭ҇тѐ*, Greek: Εὐφραίνεται ἐπὶ σοὶ ἡ Ἐκκλησία σου, Χριστέ). Based on the Canticle of Hannah (1 Sam. 2:1-2).
[^4392]: Ode 3 Troparion 1: Christ lifting up fallen nature from corruption and casting down the prince of darkness.
[^4393]: Ode 3 Troparion 2: the enemy's swords blunted and Eden reopened by the spear that pierced the side of the hypostatic Word.
[^4394]: Ode 3 Martyricon 1: rivers of demonic delusion dried up and the fire of polytheism quenched by the martyrs.
[^4395]: Ode 3 Martyricon 2: martyrs enduring crucifixion and iron claws to slay the ancient serpent with the sword of patience.
[^4396]: Ode 3 Theotokion: the immaculate Ewe-lamb lamenting at the foot of the Cross while glorifying the Master's might.
[^4397]: Ode 3 Heirmos of Second Canon (*Не мꙋ́дростїю, и҆ си́лою*, Greek: Οὐκ ἐν σοφίᾳ, καὶ δυνάμει). Boasting only in the hypostatic Wisdom of God (Jer. 9:23, 1 Sam. 2:2).
[^4398]: Ode 3 Troparion 1 (sung twice): prayer to elevate the serpent-struck mind above the desires of the flesh.
[^4399]: Ode 3 Troparion 2: seeking the advocacy of the All-immaculate Virgin at the dread trial of the Lord.
[^4400]: Ode 3 Troparion 3: transforming an unmerciful heart into compassion through the intercession of the Mother of God.
[^4401]: Ode 4 Heirmos (*Вознесе́на тѧ̀ ви́дѣвши це́рковь*, Greek: Ἐπαρθέντα σε ἰδοῦσα ἡ Ἐκκλησία). The Church standing in awe before the Sun of righteousness lifted up upon the Tree (Hab. 3:11).
[^4402]: Ode 4 Troparion 1: cosmic mourning at the voluntary Crucifixion: the sun darkened, rocks rent, and temple veil torn (Mt. 27:45, 51).
[^4403]: Ode 4 Troparion 2: the turning flaming sword (Gen. 3:24) giving way before the repentant thief at Christ's command (Lk. 23:43).
[^4404]: Ode 4 Martyricon 1: passion-bearers shielded by the Cross against the shafts of malice, casting down idolatry.
[^4405]: Ode 4 Martyricon 2: the martyrs offering themselves as pure whole-burnt offerings to Christ (Rom. 12:1).
[^4406]: Ode 4 Theotokion: the Virgin's soul pierced with anguish as she beholds her Son upon the Tree.
[^4407]: Ode 4 Troparion 1 of Second Canon (sung twice): sanctification in the divine temple of the Word Who emptied Himself (Phil. 2:7).
[^4408]: Ode 4 Troparion 2 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p448): prayer to anchor the mind tossed about by sloth and malice.
[^4409]: Ode 4 Troparion 3: transforming the den of robbers into a living palace of the Holy Trinity (Mt. 21:13, 1 Cor. 6:19).
[^4410]: Ode 5 Heirmos (*Ты̀ Гдⷭ҇и мо́й свѣ́тъ*, Greek: Σὺ Κύριέ μου φῶς). Christ as the true Light coming into the world to dispel ignorance (Jn. 8:12).
[^4411]: Ode 5 Troparion 1: streams of incorruption from Christ's pierced side healing the fall occasioned by the first-created rib (Gen. 2:21-22).
[^4412]: Ode 5 Troparion 2: the precious Cross as an invincible standard of victory over demonic powers.
[^4413]: Ode 5 Martyricon 1: martyrs passing through material flames to join the choir of angelic fiery ministers.
[^4414]: Ode 5 Martyricon 2: unbroken love of the martyrs toward the Creator despite the crushing of the flesh.
[^4415]: Ode 5 Theotokion: the Good One incarnate of the Virgin and crucified to deliver mankind from corruption.
[^4416]: Ode 5 Heirmos of Second Canon (*Нечести́вїи не ᲂу҆́зрѧтъ сла́вы твоеѧ̀*, Greek: Οἱ ἀσεβεῖς οὐκ ὄψονται). Rising early from the night to hymn the radiance of the Father's glory (Is. 26:9-10).
[^4417]: Ode 5 Troparion 1: the spiritual Ewe-lamb seeking out the lost sheep wandering upon the mountains of sin (Mt. 18:12, Lk. 15:4).
[^4418]: Ode 5 Troparion 2: warming a soul frozen in spiritual winter through the flame of divine charity.
[^4419]: Ode 5 Troparion 3: prayer to the pure among women to grant a life free from passionate blemishes.
[^4420]: Ode 5 Troparion 4: preservation under the shelter of the Virgin's wings as the apple of an eye (LXX Ps. 16:8).
[^4421]: Ode 6 Heirmos (*Пожрꙋ́ ти съ гла́сомъ хвале́нїа*, Greek: Θύσω σοι, μετὰ φωνῆς αἰνέσεως). Jonas-canticle typifying the cleansing of the Church by the Blood of Christ (Jon. 2:10).
[^4422]: Ode 6 Troparion 1: Christ enduring dishonor on the Cross to restore humanity to celestial glory.
[^4423]: Ode 6 Troparion 2: the death of Christ upon the Tree putting to death the demonic destroyer of souls.

[^4427]: Ode 6 Troparion 1 of Second Canon: the childbearing of the Theotokos destroying death and raising the slain soul.
[^4428]: Ode 6 Troparion 2: stretching forth a hand of help to the soul tossed upon the stormy sea of life.
[^4429]: Ode 6 Troparion 3: the Theotokos as the fertile field bearing the divine ear of grain (*кла́съ*, cf. Jn. 12:24) to feed hungry souls.
[^4430]: Ode 6 Troparion 4: subduing rebellious carnal passions like a wild colt through the peaceful sleep of prayer.
[^4431]: Ode 7 Heirmos (*Въ пещѝ А҆враа́мстїи ѻ҆́троцы*, Greek: Ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ Ἀβραμιαῖοι παῖδες). The Hebrew youths in the Persian furnace singing praise to the Lord of glory (Dan. 3:26-56).

[^4433]: Matins Canon of the Precious Cross, Tone 4, Ode 7 Troparion 1 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p450 in Cohort 45): Christ Who clothes the heaven with clouds (Ps. 146:8 [LXX Ps. 146:8]) stripping the enemy and clothing him in eternal shame.

[^4435]: Ode 7 Martyricon 1: the martyrs joined unto the transcendent beauty of the divine Word, despising the worldly realm amid bodily torment.
[^4436]: Ode 7 Martyricon 2: overthrowing the strongholds of pagan delusion and becoming unshakeable walls of protection for the faithful.

[^4442]: Ode 8 Heirmos (*Рꙋ́цѣ распросте́ръ Данїи́лъ*, Greek: Χεῖρας ἐκπετάσας Δανιήλ). Dual typological motif of Daniel in the lions' den (Dan. 6:22) and the Three Children in the fiery furnace (Dan. 3:57).

[^4447]: Ode 8 Theotokion (Stavrotheotokion): the Mother of God weeping at the Crucifixion while marveling at the overthrow of the enemy.

[^4450]: Ode 8 Troparion 3: breaking the cords of sin (Prov. 5:22) and cutting off insidious mental assaults.
[^4451]: Ode 8 Troparion 4: moral compunction confronting the demonic accusers at the future judgment and seeking the Mother of God's intercession.
[^4452]: Ode 9 Heirmos (*Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный*, Greek: Λίθος ἀχειρότμητος). The classic prophecy of Daniel (Dan. 2:34, 45) applied to Christ the cornerstone (Eph. 2:14, 20) uniting the divided divine and human natures.
[^4453]: Matins Canon of the Precious Cross, Tone 4, Ode 9 Troparion 1 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p452): cosmic reaction to Christ's Crucifixion (Mt. 27:45, 51)—the eclipse of the sun and trembling of the earth establishing minds in piety.
[^4454]: Ode 9 Troparion 2: contrast between the divine Lawgiver and the iniquitous sanhedrin condemning the Source of life to death on the Tree.

[^4456]: Ode 9 Martyricon 2: holy martyrs shining like luminous stars across creation, dispelling demonic darkness and bestowing healings.
[^4457]: Ode 9 Theotokion (Stavrotheotokion): invoking the Mother of God as the Cloud of Light (*Свѣ́та ѻ҆́блаче*) to dispel sinful gloom.

[^4460]: Ode 9 Troparion 3: prayer to be freed from sinful irrationality (*всѧ́кагѡ безслове́сїа грѣхо́внагѡ*) and made partaker of celestial glory.

[^4472]: Rubric for the Beatitudes (Typika) at the Divine Liturgy for Wednesday, Tone 4.

[^4476]: Beatitudes Troparion 4 (Martyricon): the holy martyrs sanctifying the earth through their athletic contests and achieving theosis by grace.
[^4477]: Beatitudes, Glory: Trinitarian doxology combining the angelic Trisagion with the prayer of the penitent thief.
[^4478]: Beatitudes, Both now (Stavrotheotokion): the Virgin lamenting the loss of Christ's radiant beauty upon the Cross and mourning the ungrateful return of the Hebrew assembly.
[^4479]: Opening of the Thursday liturgical cycle in Tone 4, dedicated to the Twelve Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia. Physical leaf p455 corresponds to printed folio 453.
[^4480]: Canonical 3-layer melodic model: Tone 4 prosomoion modeled upon *As one valiant among martyrs* (Greek: Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν μάρτυσιν, Slavonic: *Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ въ мꙋ́ченицѣхъ*).

[^4484]: Stichera to Saint Nicholas of Myra at Wednesday evening Vespers, Tone 4.
[^4485]: Sticheron 1 to Saint Nicholas: wordplay on the name of Myra (*Мѵ̑ра*) and the mystical holy chrism (*ми́ро*), perfuming the Church with virtues.
[^4486]: Sticheron 2 to Saint Nicholas: the saint as an unwaning star upon the ecclesial firmament dispelling storms and bringing spiritual serenity.
[^4487]: Sticheron 3 to Saint Nicholas: alluding to the miracle of the three innocent military commanders (*stratēlatē*) condemned to death by Emperor Constantine and rescued by Nicholas appearing in a dream.
[^4488]: Vespers Doxasticon / Theotokion: the Incarnation of the uncontainable Word assuming human nature and divinizing it.

[^4493]: Scripture: LXX Ps. 122:3-4 (*Ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς, Κύριε, ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς, ὅτι ἐπὶ πολὺ ἐπλήσθημεν ἐξουδενώσεως*).
[^4494]: Aposticha Martyricon conclusion (continued from physical leaf p456): the holy martyrs as spiritual sheep guided beside the waters of repose (LXX Ps. 22:2).
[^4495]: Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 4: petition for deliverance from afflictions and salutation of the Virgin as sole protection.

[^4497]: Wednesday Small Compline office of Tone 4. Acrostic and thematic Canon to the Most-holy Theotokos.
[^4498]: Compline Ode 1 Heirmos (*Мо́рѧ чермнꙋ́ю пꙋчи́нꙋ*, Greek: Θαλάσσης τὸ ἐρυθραῖον πέλαγος). Crossing the Red Sea and Moses' cruciform hands defeating Amalek (Ex. 14:21-29, 17:11-12).
[^4499]: Compline Ode 1 Troparion 1: dogmatic confession of the Virgin as truly and in truth (*Вои́стинꙋ и҆ въ рѣ́снотꙋ*) the Mother of God, following the definition of the Third Ecumenical Council (Ephesus, 431).

[^4503]: Compline Ode 3 Heirmos (*Не мꙋ́дростїю и҆ си́лою*, Greek: Οὐκ ἐν σοφίᾳ, καὶ δυνάμει). Canticle based on 1 Kings 2:2 [LXX 1 Sam. 2:2] and Jer. 9:23-24, boasting only in Christ the hypostatic Wisdom of God (1 Cor. 1:24).

[^4513]: Compline Ode 5 Heirmos (*Ты̀ Гдⷭ҇и мо́й свѣ́тъ*, Greek: Σὺ Κύριέ μου φῶς). Canticle of Isaiah (Isa. 26:9, Jn. 8:12), Christ the holy Light illuminating those held in ignorance.
[^4514]: Compline Ode 5 Troparion 1: the Mother of God typified as the dew of Hermon descending upon the mountains of Zion (LXX Ps. 132:3).
[^4515]: Compline Ode 5 Troparion 2: the Virgin as the spiritual paradise of Life preserving the faithful from the death of sin.

[^4517]: Compline Ode 5, Both now: restoration of fallen humanity from corruption and deliverance from spiritual abysses.
[^4518]: Compline Ode 6 Heirmos (*Пожрꙋ̀ тебѣ̀ со гла́сомъ хвале́нїѧ*, Greek: Θύσω σοι, μετὰ φωνῆς αἰνέσεως). Canticle of Jonah (Jon. 2:9-10), the Church offering a sacrifice of praise cleansed by the Blood of Christ's side.
[^4519]: Compline Ode 6 Troparion 2: drying up the torrents of impure desires through the incorrupt virginity of the Mother of God.
[^4520]: Compline Ode 6, Glory: subduing the wild colt of carnal lusts under the reins of the enlightened mind.
[^4521]: Compline Ode 6, Both now: prayer for deliverance from the terrors of the Dread Judgment and eternal damnation.

[^4524]: Compline Ode 7 Heirmos (*Въ пещѝ а҆враа́мстїи ѻ҆́троцы*, Greek: Ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ Ἀβραμιαῖοι Παῖδες). Canticle of the Three Youths (Dan. 3:28, 52-56), inflamed by love of God beyond the furnace flames.
[^4525]: Compline Ode 7 Troparion 1: the Unburnt Bush of Sinai (Ex. 3:2) consuming sinful thorns and enlightening the mind.

[^4529]: Compline Ode 8 Heirmos (*Рꙋ́цѣ распросте́ръ Данїи́лъ*, Greek: Χεῖρας ἐκπετάσας Δανιήλ). Daniel subduing the lions (Dan. 6:22) and the Three Youths extinguishing the furnace (Dan. 3:57).
[^4530]: Compline Ode 8 Troparion 1 conclusion (continued from physical leaf p459): breaking the shackles of sin and calming bodily impulses through the advocacy of the Mother of God.
[^4531]: Compline Ode 8 Troparion 2: composite Scriptural imagery of the mountain of God (LXX Ps. 67:16-17, Hab. 3:3) and deliverance from the snare of the fowler and the arrow that flies by day (LXX Ps. 90:3, 5).
[^4532]: Compline Ode 8, Glory: conceiving the fear of the Lord in the spiritual womb (Isa. 26:18) and bringing forth virtues unto angelic fellowship.
[^4533]: Compline Ode 8, Both now: opening the gates of hope and eternal life, making the faithful servant an heir of the heavenly kingdom.
[^4534]: Compline Ode 9 Heirmos (*Е҆́vа оу҆́бѡ недꙋ́гомъ преслꙋша́нїѧ*, Greek: Εὔα μὲν τῷ τῆς παρακοῆς νοσήματι). Contrast between Eve bringing the curse through disobedience (Gen. 3:16) and Mary blossoming forth universal blessing through the Incarnation.
[^4535]: Compline Ode 9 Troparion 1: petition for swift protection against the unseen assaults of demonic enemies.
[^4536]: Compline Ode 9 Troparion 2: spiritual medicine for a soul lacerated by sin, delivered by the Mother of the Word.
[^4537]: Compline Ode 9, Glory: Christ tasting death to conquer death and restore fallen Adam (Heb. 2:14-15).
[^4538]: Compline Ode 9, Both now: universal refuge in the Theotokos from the tempestuous tribulations and evil craftiness of earthly life.

[^4540]: Sessional hymn of the Holy Apostles following the First Kathisma at Thursday Matins, Tone 4.

[^4545]: Psalm 18:2 [LXX 18:1], appointed verse for the Apostles at Matins. Masoretic versification: Ps. 19:1.
[^4546]: Second Sessional hymn of the Apostles after the Second Kathisma, developing the Pauline theme of the foolishness of worldly wisdom overturned by apostolic preaching (cf. 1 Cor. 1:20-25).
[^4547]: Psalm 67:36 [LXX 67:35], standard liturgical verse accompanying the Martyricon troparion. Masoretic versification: Ps. 68:35.
[^4548]: Martyricon sessional hymn honoring the holy passion-bearers who endured physical torture for the incorruptible heavenly crown.
[^4549]: Theotokion concluding the sessional hymns of the Second Kathisma at Thursday Matins, Tone 4.

[^4552]: Theotokion concluding the sessional hymns of the Third Kathisma, supplicating the intercession of the Virgin and the Holy Apostles.
[^4553]: Title and attribution of the first canon at Thursday Matins, composed by Saint Theophanes the Branded (ку́ръ Ѳеофа́на / τοῦ κυροῦ Θεοφάνους), Metropolitan of Nicaea (c. 778–845).
[^4554]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Canon of the Holy Apostles, Tone 4. The hymn recalls Moses' victory over Amalek through the cruciform extension of his hands in the wilderness of Rephidim (Exodus 17:8-16).
[^4555]: First troparion of Ode 1, Canon of the Apostles, portraying the Apostles as divine musical instruments animated by the breath of the Holy Spirit.
[^4556]: Second troparion of Ode 1, Canon of the Apostles, an individual penitential supplication seeking the apostolic visitation to cure spiritual indolence.
[^4557]: Third troparion of Ode 1, Canon of the Apostles, contrasting the irrationality (безслове́сіе / ἀλογία) of gentile delusion with the divine reason of the incarnate Word.
[^4558]: Theotokion of Ode 1, Canon of the Apostles, confessing Mary's perpetual virginity before, during, and after childbirth (*aeiparthenia*).
[^4559]: Title and acrostic rubric for the second canon of Thursday Matins, dedicated to Saint Nicholas of Myra and composed by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886). The acrostic in Slavonic is: *Четве́ртое сїѐ на Нїкола́а плеска́ніе* (representing the Greek: Τέταρτον τόδε Νικολάου κρότημα. Ἰωσήφ), meaning "This fourth clapping of hands [hymn of praise] unto Nicholas."
[^4560]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Canon to Saint Nicholas, Tone 4 (Τριστάτας κραταιούς / Трїста́ты крѣ̑пкіѧ). The hymn splits across leaves p462 and p463.
[^4561]: Conclusion of the Heirmos of Ode 1, Canon to Saint Nicholas, from leaf p462. The Patristic imagery refers to the tripartite structure of the human soul (rational, irascible, and concupiscent — τὸ τριμερές / трича́стное) drowned in the sea of passionlessness, echoing the drowning of Pharaoh's captains (Exodus 15:4).
[^4562]: First troparion of Ode 1, Canon to Saint Nicholas, petitioning the saint to banish sorrow through spiritual joy.
[^4563]: Second troparion of Ode 1, Canon to Saint Nicholas, utilizing the Gospel metaphor of the lamp placed upon a candlestick (Matthew 5:15).
[^4564]: Third troparion of Ode 1, Canon to Saint Nicholas, invoking the hierarch as a celestial helmsman guiding the soul across the stormy sea of worldly existence.
[^4565]: Theotokion of Ode 1, Canon to Saint Nicholas, praying for spiritual wakefulness against demonic slumber.

[^4594]: First troparion of Ode 5, Canon to Saint Nicholas, comparing Nicholas' earthly departure to the setting sun and his heavenly glorification to radiant daylight.

[^4627]: First troparion of Ode 8, Canon to Saint Nicholas, commemorating Saint Nicholas' defense of Orthodox dogma and condemnation of the Arian heresy at the First Ecumenical Council of Nicaea (325 AD).
[^4628]: Second troparion of Ode 8, Canon to Saint Nicholas, playing on the Greek etymology of the name Nicholas (*Nikolaos*, from *nikē*, "victory", and *laos*, "people" — "conqueror of the people" / "victory of the people").
[^4629]: Third troparion of Ode 8, Canon to Saint Nicholas, pleading for the saint's assistance in times of urgent necessity.
[^4630]: Triadicon of Ode 8, Canon to Saint Nicholas, a trinitarian doxology confessing the consubstantial Trinity in orthodox worship.
[^4631]: Theotokion of Ode 8, Canon to Saint Nicholas, invoking the Virgin's protection at the dread Dread Judgment.
[^4632]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Canon of the Holy Apostles, Tone 4 (Λίθος ἀχειρότμητος / Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный), based on Daniel's prophetic vision of the stone cut without hands from the mountain (Daniel 2:34-35, 44-45), typifying the Virgin Birth.
[^4633]: First troparion of Ode 9, Canon of the Apostles, styling the Apostles as living chosen stones building up the Church upon Christ the cornerstone (1 Peter 2:4-6; Ephesians 2:20).

[^4637]: Theotokion of Ode 9, Canon of the Apostles.

[^4639]: First troparion of Ode 9, Canon to Saint Nicholas, celebrating the continuous flow of miraculous, healing myrrh (*myron*) exuding from the holy relics of Saint Nicholas in Myra (and subsequently Bari).
[^4640]: Second troparion of Ode 9, Canon to Saint Nicholas, comparing Nicholas' universal illumination of the cosmos to the solar rays.
[^4641]: Third troparion of Ode 9, Canon to Saint Nicholas, imploring habitual pastoral mercy against the triple onslaught of worldly distress, demonic illusion, and wicked slanderers.

[^4643]: Theotokion of Ode 9, Canon to Saint Nicholas, weaving the angelic greeting of Archangel Gabriel (Luke 1:28) with the typological figure of the garden containing the Tree of Life (Genesis 2:9).

[^4769]: ODE 4 (Canon 2, Troparion 1). The spiritual dawn of repentance dispelling the gloom of sin is a persistent patristic motif in the Octoechos hymnography.
[^4770]: ODE 4 (Canon 2, Troparion 2). Slavonic *ѿ чꙋжда́гѡ* ("from the stranger") translates Greek ἐκ τοῦ ἀλλοτρίου, referring to the adversary or demonic alien power.

[^4773]: ODE 5. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Оу҆жасо́шасѧ всѧ́ческая ѡ҆ боже́ственнѣй сла́вѣ тво́ей...* Greek: Ἐξέστη τὰ σύμπαντα ἐπὶ τῇ θείᾳ δόξῃ σου.
[^4774]: ODE 5 (Canon 1, Troparion 1). The miraculous darkening of the physical sun at the Crucifixion of Christ; cf. Matt. 27:45, Luke 23:44-45.
[^4775]: ODE 5 (Canon 1, Troparion 2). The tasting of vinegar and gall (Matt. 27:34) healing Adam's tasting of the forbidden fruit in Eden (Gen. 3:6).
[^4776]: ODE 5 (Canon 1, Martyricon 1). Metaphor of pagan persecution as "the winter of delusion" dispelled by the warmth of the Holy Spirit.
[^4777]: ODE 5 (Canon 1, Martyricon 2). "The water of life" (LXX Ps. 22:2, Apoc. 22:1).
[^4778]: ODE 5 (Canon 1, Stavrotheotokion). The lamentation of the Mother of God at the Tree of the Cross.
[^4779]: ODE 5 (Canon 2, Troparion 1, incipit). Stanza continues on physical leaf p482 (fol. 480r).
[^4780]: ODE 5 (Canon 2, Troparion 1, conclusion). Conclusion of stanza from physical leaf p481 (fol. 479v).
[^4781]: ODE 5 (Canon 2, Troparion 2). "Before the fearful day" refers to the Day of Judgment (cf. Zeph. 1:14-15, 2 Pet. 3:10).
[^4782]: ODE 5 (Canon 2, Troparion 3). The healing of contrition/crushing of the mind (*сокруше́ніѧ оу҆ма̀*) and acedia/despondency (*оу҆ны́ніе*).
[^4783]: ODE 5 (Canon 2, Troparion 4). Slavonic *шата̑ніѧ* ("insolence" / "rage"), Greek φρυάγματα; cf. LXX Ps. 2:1.
[^4784]: ODE 6. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Бж҃е́ственное сїѐ и҆ всечестно́е соверша́юще пра́зднество...* Greek: Τὴν θείαν ταύτην καὶ πάνтиμον τελοῦντες ἑορτήν.
[^4785]: ODE 6 (Canon 1, Troparion 1). The Christological paradox of Christ's voluntary passion putting an end to human pain (*бѡлѣ́зни оу҆ста́вилъ є҆сѝ*).
[^4786]: ODE 6 (Canon 1, Troparion 2). The rending of the veil of the temple (*церко́внаѧ свѣ́тлость*) and splitting of the rocks at the Crucifixion (Matt. 27:51).
[^4787]: ODE 6 (Canon 1, Martyricon 1). The ancient serpent (the devil) slain through the victorious death of the martyrs (Apoc. 12:9-11).
[^4788]: ODE 6 (Canon 1, Martyricon 2). The martyrs receiving incorruptible crowns (1 Cor. 9:25).
[^4789]: ODE 6 (Canon 1, Theotokion). The Theotokos as the spiritual haven (*приста́нище*) for souls navigating the storms of life.
[^4790]: ODE 6 (Canon 2, Heirmos). Jonah prefiguring the three-day burial and resurrection of Christ; cf. Jonah 2:1-10, Matt. 12:40. Note that Canon 2 here introduces its own distinct Heirmos (*Возопѝ, проѡбразꙋ́ѧ...*).
[^4791]: ODE 6 (Canon 2, Troparion 1, incipit). Stanza continues on physical leaf p483 (fol. 481r).
[^4792]: ODE 6 (Canon 2, Troparion 1, conclusion). Conclusion of stanza from physical leaf p482 (fol. 480v). The title "Cloud of the Sun of righteousness" evokes Mal. 4:2 and Isa. 19:1.
[^4793]: ODE 6 (Canon 2, Troparion 2). The haven (*приста́нище*) calming the tempest of sinful thoughts.
[^4794]: ODE 6 (Canon 2, Troparion 3). Tears of compunction extinguishing the unquenchable fire of Gehenna (cf. Mark 9:43-48).
[^4795]: ODE 6 (Canon 2, Troparion 4). The antithesis between the deep of sins (*ѕѡ́лъ пꙋчи́на*) and the Virgin who bore the abyss of divine goodness (*бла́гости пꙋчи́на*).
[^4796]: ODE 7. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Не послꙋжи́ша тва́ри бгомоу́дріи...* Greek: Οὐκ ἐλάτреυσαν τῇ κτίσει οἱ θεόφρονες; cf. Rom. 1:25, Dan. 3:28.
[^4797]: ODE 7 (Canon 1, Troparion 1). The casting down of Satan by the Cross; cf. John 12:31, Col. 2:15.
[^4798]: ODE 7 (Canon 1, Troparion 2). By Christ's wounds we are healed (Isa. 53:5, 1 Pet. 2:24).
[^4799]: ODE 7 (Canon 1, Martyricon 1). The charismatic gift of healings granted to the martyrs (1 Cor. 12:9).
[^4800]: ODE 7 (Canon 1, Martyricon 2). Slavonic *кори́тель* ("tormentor" / "insulter"), Greek ὑβριστής.
[^4801]: ODE 7 (Canon 1, Stavrotheotokion). Marian types: the untilled earth (*землѧ̀ недѣ́ланнаѧ*), palace (*пала́та*), and fiery throne (*престо́лъ ѻ҆гнезра́ченъ*); deification of humanity (*ѡ҆боже́ніе* / theosis).
[^4802]: ODE 7 (Canon 2, Heirmos). The Three Children delivered in Babylon while the flame slew the Chaldean executioners; Dan. 3:22-26.
[^4803]: ODE 7 (Canon 2, Troparion 1, incipit). Stanza continues on physical leaf p484 (fol. 482r).

[^4805]: ODE 7 (Canon 2, Troparion 2). Allusion to LXX Ps. 22:4: "For though I should walk in the midst of the shadow of death, I will fear no evils: for Thou art with me."
[^4806]: ODE 7 (Canon 2, Troparion 3). The Mother of God as the throne of the Most High (Isa. 66:1).
[^4807]: ODE 7 (Canon 2, Troparion 4). Allusion to LXX Ps. 90:3: "For He shall deliver thee from the snare of the hunters."
[^4808]: ODE 8. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Ѻ҆́троки благочести̑выѧ въ пещѝ рождество̀ Бг҃оро́дично спасло́ є҆́сть...* Greek: Παῖδας εὐαγεῖς ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ; Dan. 3:57.
[^4809]: ODE 8 (Canon 1, Troparion 1). The Christological paradox of the Timeless One (*безлѣ́тный*) entering into time (*въ лѣ́то бы́въ*) to unloose temporal bonds.
[^4810]: ODE 8 (Canon 1, Troparion 2). Allusion to Col. 2:15: "And having spoiled principalities and powers, He made a show of them openly, triumphing over them in it."
[^4811]: ODE 8 (Canon 1, Martyricon 1). Martyrs mounting their own blood as a chariot, reminiscent of Elijah's ascent (4 Reg. 2:11).
[^4812]: ODE 8 (Canon 1, Martyricon 2). Torments of the martyrs passing through fire and water (cf. LXX Ps. 65:12).
[^4813]: ODE 8 (Canon 1, Stavrotheotokion). The sleep of Christ upon the Cross awakening mankind from spiritual slumber; cf. LXX Ps. 3:5, 43:23.
[^4814]: ODE 8 (Canon 2, Troparion 1). The soul trembling before the Dread Judgment Seat (*сꙋди́ще*); cf. 2 Cor. 5:10.
[^4815]: ODE 8 (Canon 2, Troparion 2). The Virgin Mother as constant recourse against demonic assault.
[^4816]: ODE 8 (Canon 2, Troparion 3). Classical Byzantine eschatological doctrine of the aerial toll-houses (*мыта́рства*) and the demonic prince of the air (Eph. 2:2) encountered at the soul's departure.
[^4817]: ODE 8 (Canon 2, Troparion 4). The Mother of God as the universal champion and protectress (*предста́тельница*) of humankind.
[^4818]: The Magnificat (Luke 1:46-55), sung with prostrations before Ode 9 at Matins.

[^4820]: ODE 9 (Canon 1, Troparion 1). The crown of thorns (Matt. 27:29) reversing the curse of thorns and thistles brought about by Adam's disobedience (Gen. 3:18).
[^4821]: ODE 9 (Canon 1, Troparion 2). Cosmic phenomena at the Crucifixion (Matt. 27:45, 51).

[^4825]: The variable commemorative name rubric *І҆мпера́торꙋ (И҆́мⷶкъ)* ("Emperor [Name]") historically designates the reigning Austro-Hungarian monarch, Emperor Franz Joseph I (reigned 1848–1916), under whose imperial approbation this 1895 Lviv Stauropegion Octoechos was published.

[^4850]: "Lord, I have cried," Sticheron 1, Tone 4. The martyrs enduring bodily torment in view of celestial beatitude.
[^4851]: Liturgical rubric directing the repetition of the first sticheron when six or ten stichera are appointed.
[^4852]: "Lord, I have cried," Sticheron 2. Hierarchs as shepherds imitating the Good Shepherd (John 10:11-12) defending their flock against grievous wolves (Acts 20:29).
[^4853]: "Lord, I have cried," Sticheron 3. The martyrs as "rational whole-burnt offerings" (*всесожже́нїа слове́снаѧ*, Rom. 12:1) pastured by the "water of rest" (LXX Ps. 22:2).

[^4855]: "Lord, I have cried," Sticheron 4, conclusion. Conclusion of stanza from physical leaf p488 (fol. 486v).
[^4856]: "Lord, I have cried," Sticheron 5. The boldness (*дерзнове́ніе*) of the saints before Christ; cf. 1 John 3:21.
[^4857]: DOGMATIKON / THEOTOKION OF TONE 4: The classical Friday evening / Saturday Vespers Dogmatic Theotokion by St. John of Damascus (*И҆́же тебѐ ра́ди бг҃ооте́цъ проро́къ Дави́дъ...*), weaving together Psalm 44:10 (LXX: "at Thy right hand stood the queen in a vesture of inwoven gold"), the Incarnation restoring the fallen divine image in man (Gen. 1:26-27), and the parable of the lost sheep carried upon the Good Shepherd's shoulder (Luke 15:4-7). Slavonic *горохи́щное ѡ҆вча̀* renders Greek ὀρειάλωτον πρόβατον ("the sheep caught upon the mountains").
[^4858]: Order of Vespers: Hymn *Свѣ́те ти́хїй* (Phos Hilaron), Friday evening Prokeimenon (*Боже, заступникъ мой еси*), prayer *Vouchsafe, O Lord* (*Сподо́би Гдⷭ҇и*), and Augmented / Evening Litany.
[^4859]: Aposticha to the Martyrs, Sticheron 1, Tone 4.
[^4860]: Psalm verse from LXX Ps. 122:1-2.
[^4861]: Aposticha to the Martyrs, Sticheron 2.
[^4862]: Psalm verse from LXX Ps. 122:3-4.
[^4863]: Aposticha, Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) (*Ме́ртвенъ* / For the Departed), Tone 4. The classical troparion of the Panikhida / Parastas (*Со дꙋ́хи пра́ведныхъ...*); cf. Heb. 12:23 ("the spirits of just men made perfect").
[^4864]: Aposticha, Theotokion.
[^4865]: Vespers concluding order: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29-32), Trisagion prayers, Dismissal Troparion of Saturday (*А҆по́столи, Мꙋ́ченицы и҆ Проро́цы...*), and Dismissal.
[^4866]: COMPLINE CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS, ODE 1. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Трїста́ты крѣ́пкїѧ, рожде́йся ѿ Дѣ́вы...* Greek: Θαλάσσης τὸ ἐρυθраῖον πέλαγος; cf. Exod. 14:28, 15:4. The mystical allegorization of the drowning of Pharaoh's "three captains" (*триста́ты*, Exod. 14:7) as the subjection of the tripartite soul (rational, irascible, and appetitive / *дꙋшѝ трича́стное* in Platonic-Patristic psychology) unto the depth of divine dispassion (*безстра́стїе* / apatheia).
[^4867]: The liturgical refrain (*припѣ́въ*) to the Theotokos used between troparia in the canon.
[^4868]: COMPLINE CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS, ODE 1, Troparion 1. Deliverance from the "mire of passions" (*ка́лъ страсте́й*) and the fiery darts of the devil (Eph. 6:16).
[^4869]: ODE 1, Troparion 2. The light of the divine commandments illuminating the dawn of repentance.
[^4870]: ODE 1, Troparion 3 (*Glory...*). Sinking into the "evil mire" (*ти́на лꙋка́ваѧ*); cf. LXX Ps. 68:3 ("I am stuck fast in the mire of the deep, and there is no standing").
[^4871]: ODE 1, Troparion 4 (*Both now...*). Christ as the fountain of incorruption abolishing death and corruption through His Nativity.
[^4872]: ODE 3. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Не мꙋ́дростїю, и҆ си́лою, и҆ бога́тствомъ хва́лимся...* Greek: Οὐκ ἐν σοφίᾳ, καὶ δυνάμει καὶ πλούτῳ καυχώмеθα; cf. 1 Reg. (1 Sam.) 2:2, Jer. 9:23-24, 1 Cor. 1:30-31.
[^4873]: ODE 3, Troparion 1. Deliverance from the darkness and chains of sin.
[^4874]: ODE 3, Troparion 2. Establishing the trembling heart in the chaste fear of God (LXX Ps. 18:10).
[^4875]: ODE 3, Troparion 3 (*Glory...*). Recourse to the Virgin's mantle when all human aid fails.
[^4876]: ODE 3, Troparion 4 (*Both now...*). The Mother of God arrayed in fringes of gold (*ра́сны златы́ми*) at the right hand of Christ; LXX Ps. 44:10, 14.
[^4877]: ODE 4. Heirmos. Slavonic: *Сѣдѧ́й въ сла́вѣ, на престо́лѣ Божества̀...* Greek: Ὁ καθήμενος ἐν δόξῃ ἐπὶ θρόνου θεότητος; cf. Isa. 19:1, Hab. 3:2.
[^4878]: ODE 4, Troparion 1. Slavonic *присво́й твоемꙋ̀ Сы́нꙋ* ("unite / reconcile me unto Thy Son"), Greek οἰκείωσον.

[^4885]: ODE 5 (Troparion 3). Allusion to LXX Ps. 16:8: "Keep me as the apple of the eye; hide me under the shadow of Thy wings."

[^5034]: BEATITUDES (Troparion 2, Martyrs). Second Beatitude troparion for the Holy Martyrs, Tone 4.
[^5035]: BEATITUDES (Troparion 3, All Saints). Third Beatitude troparion, commemorating hierarchs, prophets, venereables, and holy women.
[^5036]: BEATITUDES (Troparion 4, Departed). Fourth Beatitude troparion for the departed, Tone 4; Isaiah 35:10.
[^5037]: BEATITUDES (Glory, Trinity). Trinitarian troparion (`Сла́ва:`) at the Beatitudes, confessing the indivisible and tri-hypostatic Trinity.
[^5038]: BEATITUDES (Both now, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) at the Beatitudes, confessing the two wills and two operations of Christ according to the Sixth Ecumenical Council; Psalm 109:3 [LXX].
[^5039]: Scribal colophon marking the conclusion of Tone 4.
